<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_015Fx6ZKaoZDEimxHvTVtWm4`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:introduction}
3	
4	GRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on first-order logic problems ($97{\pm}2\%$ across three seeds, 1000 steps), yet at convergence produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs.
5	Each seed develops a distinct degenerate shortcut: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, or tautological collapse, all invisible to execution-based evaluation, which registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation.
6	
7	\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}
8	Prove rate, the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}, conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.
9	Recent work applies GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on structural quality.
10	Process reward models~\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.
11	A separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.
12	
13	\paragraph{Solver Trace Ablation.}
14	We address this gap with \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), which applies \emph{component necessity testing} (leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs) to quantify structural integrity (Figure~\ref{fig:sta-overview}).
15	For each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \emph{Irreducible Solver Rate} (ISR), with $\text{ISR}{=}1$ indicating full necessity and $\text{ISR}{=}0$ complete redundancy.
16	LOO ablation itself is standard~\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; our contribution lies in applying it as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and in the empirical phenomena it surfaces.
17	ISR captures structural rather than semantic integrity (downstream NLI: $\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$).
18	As a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\citep{folio}, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR: $\text{ISR}^{*}{:}\,0.642\to 0.132\to 0.003$ over 100--1000 steps (seed~42; 99.5\% degradation) while PR rises $32\%{\to}96\%$.
19	Three seeds diverge radically (PR $\sigma{=}47$\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different mode: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse.
20	An SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\text{PR}{=}13.0\%$ with 92.3\% non-hacking (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps), consistent with the failure being RL-specific rather than data- or capacity-driven.
21	
22	\paragraph{ISR as reward.}
23	Repurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\text{exec}} \cdot (1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, ISR contains an inherent structural vulnerability: tautological proofs (a single premise identical to the conclusion) receive $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$ by construction, and implication tautologies ($P{\to}P$) receive $\text{ISR}{=}0.75$, creating a positive feedback loop once sampled during GRPO rollouts~\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, kwa-catastrophic-goodhart}.
24	Goodhart collapse~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} is observed at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\beta{=}0.1$): $\beta$ controls \emph{when} collapse occurs, not \emph{whether}.
25	At moderate doses, collapse is stochastic and delayed: seeds bifurcate between rapid convergence to tautology and a transient genuine window that closes under extended training.
26	Snapshot evaluation would overestimate ISR's benefit; trajectory analysis confirms the window is temporary.
27	The effect transfers to Prolog (3/3 seeds yield 0\% non-hacking via distinct mechanisms, including \emph{query-as-fact gaming}) and is preliminarily observed at 1.7B scale (qualitatively novel gaming modes in single-seed evaluation).
28	ISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \emph{reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer at any dose.}
29	
30	\paragraph{Contributions.}
31	Across 6 reward conditions, 5 seeds, 2 logic types, 2 model scales, and up to 1000 steps, we contribute: (\textbf{C1}) We empirically demonstrate ISR as a diagnostic for GRPO reward hacking, revealing a 99.5\% collapse trajectory invisible to PR, with an SFT control consistent with RL-specific failure (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\textbf{C2}) an empirical instance of Goodhart collapse~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} at all four tested doses: ISR's structural bias ($\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$ for tautological proofs) creates a positive feedback loop; the genuine window at moderate $\beta$ is suggestive but non-robust, and $\beta$ controls only timing, not outcome (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); (\textbf{C3}) cross-logic transfer to Prolog with consistent Goodhart effects across all seeds (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}); (\textbf{C4}) a nine-mode hacking taxonomy including query-as-fact gaming, obfuscated tautology, and a scale-specific \emph{ex falso} mode, motivating functional cross-validation of diagnostics (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
32	These are cautionary results: snapshot evaluation of structural rewards can overestimate their benefit; only trajectory analysis reveals the transient nature of apparent improvement.
33	
```

### Tool result

_source line 9 · 2026-05-17T18:35:02.424Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01ArxPfxDijWXVY77B2FHnSs`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	GRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on FOLIO ($97{\pm}2\%$) yet produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs at convergence, a failure invisible to execution-based evaluation.
3	We propose \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a leave-one-out necessity test on solver inputs whose derived metric, Irreducible Solver Rate (ISR), exposes a 99.5\% structural collapse hidden behind rising prove rates; a supervised baseline achieves 92\% non-hacking on identical data, suggesting the failure is RL-specific.
4	Repurposed as a reward signal, ISR exhibits Goodhart collapse at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\beta{=}0.1$): tautological proofs receive artificially high ISR by construction, and the transient genuine window at moderate doses shows suggestive but non-robust improvement over random controls that disappears under extended training.
5	Across multiple reward conditions, logic types, and model scales, we catalog nine hacking modes and document that ISR is reliable as a diagnostic but unreliable as an optimization target.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
6	\end{abstract}
7	
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Bxfytz7WdBGPYg2ejnvDa6`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Experiments}
2	\label{sec:experiments}
3	
4	We evaluate STA along three axes: ISR as a diagnostic for reward hacking (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}); ISR as a training reward across four dose levels, where it exhibits Goodhart effects (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}); and STA as a cross-logic protocol (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
5	Cross-scale evidence from Qwen3-1.7B reveals capacity-dependent Goodhart basins (\S\ref{sec:crossscale}).
6	A hacking taxonomy synthesizes the modes observed across conditions (\S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
7	
8	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
9	
10	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline is trained with negative log-likelihood (NLL) on gold formalizations.\footnote{All experiments use \texttt{data/train\_500\_seed42.jsonl}; seeds control initialization and rollout sampling only.}
11	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples (\texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_100.jsonl}) with greedy decoding (Appendix~\ref{app:setup}).
12	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for H1/H7 plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate, defined as the residual of proved samples matching none of the nine cataloged patterns (a necessary but not sufficient condition for semantic correctness).
13	
14	\subsection{ISR as Diagnostic}
15	\label{sec:diagnostic}
16	
17	\begin{figure}[t]
18	\centering
19	\includegraphics[width=0.95\columnwidth]{figures/fig2_isr_collapse.pdf}
20	\caption{ISR collapse under exec-only GRPO (seed~42). PR rises 32\%$\to$93\%$\to$96\% while proved-only ISR falls $0.642{\to}0.132{\to}0.003$ over 100$\to$500$\to$1000 steps. By step 1000, 94.8\% of proved samples have $\text{ISR}{=}0$ and mean component count inflates 6.58$\to$18.55 (contradiction explosion).}
21	\label{fig:isr-collapse}
22	\end{figure}
23	
24	\textbf{Collapse trajectory.}
25	Figure~\ref{fig:isr-collapse} traces exec-only GRPO (seed~42) at 100, 500, 1000 steps.
26	PR rises $32\%\to93\%\to96\%$, a trajectory that execution-only evaluation would interpret as successful optimization; $\text{ISR}^*$ tells the opposite story, $0.642\to0.132\to0.003$ (214$\times$ collapse). %% registry: plan003-eval-exec-only-r5, plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan016-extended-1000-s42
27	By 500 steps, 87.1\% of proved samples embed the conclusion (H1); by 1000 steps, the embedding rate saturates at 100\% and mean component count more than doubles ($6.58\to18.55$) as the model emits contradictory or vacuous premises that trivially derive any conclusion.
28	Extended training, lacking a structural signal, \emph{worsens} formalization quality while improving the headline number.
29	
30	\textbf{Cross-seed instability.}
31	The three exec-only seeds diverge into qualitatively different failure modes despite shared data, hyperparameters, and reward (Appendix~\ref{app:detailed-results}): s42 reaches $\text{PR}{=}93\%$ via conclusion embedding ($\text{H1}{=}87\%$); s43 collapses to $\text{PR}{=}13\%$ (at 500 steps) via repetition (87\% of rollouts truncated); s44 reaches $\text{PR}{=}98\%$ via tautological collapse ($\text{H7}{=}100\%$, mean $n{=}2.04$). %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2, plan001-exec-only-s43-r4, plan001-exec-only-s44
32	The cross-seed PR variance ($\sigma{=}47$\,pp) shows that aggregate metrics obscure qualitatively different failure modes; all three are flagged by $\text{ISR}^*$ even though one passes the PR check (s44) and one fails it (s43).
33	By 1000 steps, all three converge to high PR ($0.97{\pm}0.02$) with near-zero genuine output (see Appendix~\ref{app:h8} and Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed}), each through a different dominant mode (s42: $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.003$, taxonomy unavailable; s43: H1 $93.8\%$, genuine $6.2\%$; s44: H1 $85\%$ + H8 $15\%$). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42, exec-only-s43-1000step-r7, plan001-exec-only-s44-1000-r2
34	S43 recovers from $\text{PR}{=}13\%$ (500 steps) to $\text{PR}{=}96\%$ (1000 steps), but entirely through H1 exploitation: PR recovery does not imply quality recovery, and the repetition-collapse phase is transient rather than terminal.
35	
36	\textbf{SFT baseline.}
37	SFT on gold formalizations reaches $\text{PR}{=}13.0\%$ (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps) with $\text{H7}{=}0$, $\text{H1}{=}7.7\%$, $92.3\%$ non-hacking ($n{=}39$; $\text{OR}{=}147$ vs.\ exec-only, $p{<}0.001$; per-seed range ${\sim}$91--93\%). %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3
38	The near-absence of hacking under identical data exposure is consistent with hacking being RL-specific; a 100-step checkpoint ($\text{PR}{=}32\%$, hacking${\geq}31\%$) partially controls for the PR-level confound, though the PR gap remains substantial (Appendix~\ref{app:100step}).
39	SFT trains via negative log-likelihood on gold formalizations without exploration-exploitation dynamics; exploit discovery requires the trial-and-error sampling inherent to RL but absent from supervised training.
40	Whether SFT would develop similar exploits at higher prove rates remains an open question; this mechanistic argument is plausible but not conclusive.
41	
42	\subsection{ISR as Reward: Goodhart Effects}
43	\label{sec:goodhart}
44	
45	We now reverse roles: instead of treating ISR as an observer, we use it as a reward augmentation, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,\text{ISR})$, with a matched random control, $r = r_{\text{exec}}\cdot(1 + \beta\,u)$, $u\sim\mathcal{U}(0,1)$, to isolate ISR-specific signal from generic reward-variance effects.
46	We present $\beta{=}0.1$ first as the focal dose (5 seeds, trajectory analysis), then examine lower and higher doses.
47	The headline finding: ISR contains an inherent Goodhart vulnerability~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} observed to produce collapse at all four tested doses ($\beta\in\{0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3\}$).
48	The mechanism is structural: tautological proofs ($P{\to}P$) receive $\text{ISR}{=}0.75$ and single-premise-equals-conclusion constructions receive $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$, both by the LOO definition alone.
49	Once sampled during GRPO rollouts, these high-ISR tautologies create a positive feedback loop; $\beta$ controls only the \emph{timing} of collapse, not whether it occurs.
50	
51	\begin{table}[t]
52	\centering
53	\small
54	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
55	\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}
56	\toprule
57	\textbf{Condition} & $\boldsymbol{\beta}$ & \textbf{PR\%}$\uparrow$ & \textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \textbf{H7\%} & \textbf{H1\%} & \textbf{N-H\%} \\
58	\midrule
59	Base (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\ %% registry: sta-phase0-baseline
60	SFT & --- & 13 & 0.595 & 0 & 8 & \textbf{92} \\ %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3 (3-seed pooled)
61	\midrule
62	Exec-only$^\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 8 \\ %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2 (H8 corrected)
63	Exec-only (1k)$^{\dagger\dagger}$ & --- & 97{\scriptsize$\pm$2} & 0.03{\scriptsize$\pm$.05} & 0 & --- & $\leq$2$^{\dagger\dagger}$ \\
64	ISR$^\ddagger$ & 0.1 & 70{\scriptsize$\pm$19} & --- & \textbf{54} & 18 & 23$^\star$ \\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44/s45/s46 (5-seed, D124)
65	Random & 0.1 & \textbf{83}{\scriptsize$\pm$15} & 0.080{\scriptsize$\pm$.06} & 30 & 59 & 8 \\ %% registry: plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r3/s44-r2/s45/s46 (5-seed)
66	ISR & 0.2 & 97{\scriptsize$\pm$4} & 0.69{\scriptsize$\pm$.24} & \textbf{99} & 0 & 0 \\ %% registry: sweep-isr-b02-s42/s43/s44 (3-seed; Appendix Table~\ref{tab:beta02-detail})
67	\midrule
68	ISR & 0.3 & 66{\scriptsize$\pm$42} & 0.39{\scriptsize$\pm$.31} & \textbf{50} & 37 & 11 \\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44 (pooled; D106 s43 genuine 23→20)
69	Random & 0.3 & 54{\scriptsize$\pm$35} & 0.09{\scriptsize$\pm$.09} & 7 & 91 & 2 \\ %% registry: plan017-additive-random-s42, exp1-random-b03-s43-r4, exp1-random-b03-s44-r2 (pooled; D086 H7: s42=4,s43=7,s44=0)
70	\midrule
71	\multicolumn{7}{l}{\textit{Qwen3-1.7B (seed~42)}} \\
72	ISR & 0.1 & 100 & 0.600 & 0 & \textbf{100} & 0 \\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-isr-b01-s42
73	Random & 0.1 & 93 & 0.028 & 0 & 0 & 0 \\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-random-b01-s42 (H9=100%)
74	ISR & 0.3 & 17 & 0.031 & 0 & 71 & 29 \\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-isr-b03-s42
75	\bottomrule
76	\end{tabular}}
77	\caption{Goodhart landscape. PR/ISR$^*$: mean$\pm$std; H7/H1/N-H: pooled \%. $^\dagger$s42, 500 steps. $^\ddagger$N-H uncorrected upper bound (s43/s44 H8 audit unavailable). $^\star$Transient; collapses at 1000 steps (\S\ref{sec:goodhart-temporal}). $^{\dagger\dagger}$3-seed 1000-step (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:exec-only-1000}).}
78	\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}
79	\end{table}
80	
81	
82	\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.1$): stochastic delayed collapse.}
83	At $\beta{=}0.1$, 5 seeds exhibit a striking bifurcation: 2 seeds (s43, s44) collapse rapidly to near-pure H7 tautology (${\geq}97\%$ H7, $0\%$ genuine within ${\sim}150$ steps), while 3 seeds (s42, s45, s46) sustain a transient genuine window.
84	The 5-seed pooled non-hacking rate is ${\sim}23\%$ at 500 steps, with the collapsed seeds pulling down the average; the 3 non-collapsed seeds individually range from $17\%$ to $55\%$ genuine.
85	This bifurcation is stochastic: all seeds are indistinguishable in the first ${\sim}100$ steps ($\text{ISR}{\approx}0.05$--$0.07$); divergence occurs at steps 150--200 when H7 tautologies, which receive $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$ by construction, are sampled during rollouts and reinforced via the positive feedback loop.
86	Once a seed enters the H7 basin, convergence to saturation takes ${\sim}150$ steps and is irreversible. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44/s45/s46
87	
88	H7 (a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\text{ISR}\geq 0.5$ by the LOO definition; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}) is the canonical Goodhart exploit: the model satisfies the structural metric directly rather than producing a genuine derivation.
89	Total conclusion embedding ($\text{H1}+\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\%$ across both ISR and random conditions (Appendix Figure~\ref{fig:hacking-composition}); the specific mode (H7 vs.\ H1) within a seed is stochastic under both conditions ($\text{CV}{>}1$), though ISR modestly increases H7 probability by providing a direct reward pathway.
90	Random yields higher PR ($83{\pm}15\%$ vs.\ $70{\pm}19\%$; $n{=}5$ each).
91	Random H7 exhibits extreme seed dependence (per-seed range: $0\%$--$91.5\%$; pooled $30\%$ is dominated by s46 at $91.5\%$ and s43 at $38.3\%$).
92	Both conditions produce H7 in 2 of 5 seeds, but the quality differs: ISR-induced H7 is absorbing ($100\%$ of proved samples in affected seeds: s43${=}61/61$, s44${=}89/89$), while random-induced H7 coexists with other modes ($38$--$92\%$).
93	ISR's reward signal converts H7 from a stochastic possibility into an irreversible attractor once sampled.
94	The reward landscape explains H7's attractor strength: H7 tautologies receive expected reward $1{+}0.75\beta$ versus ${\sim}1{+}0.26\beta$ for genuine proofs (premise-count-matched), a gap of ${\sim}0.49\beta$ that creates a persistent gradient toward tautological constructions regardless of dose.
95	
96	\textbf{The transient genuine window.}
97	At 500 steps, ISR produces more genuine output than random controls (pooled N-H: 23\% vs.\ 8\%), with 3 ISR seeds showing ${\geq}17\%$ genuine (per-seed range: 17\%--55\%) versus predominantly H1-dominated random seeds, a descriptive pattern consistent with ISR temporarily rewarding necessary premises before H7 dominates.
98	However, statistical inference is limited: Cochran--Mantel--Haenszel tests yield $\text{OR}_{\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$ ($p{<}10^{-9}$), but within-seed samples share a checkpoint and use greedy decoding, violating the independence assumption required by CMH; the effective sample size is seeds ($n{=}5$), not samples ($n{=}350$/$414$).
99	Seed-level permutation tests, which correctly treat seeds as the unit of observation, yield $p{=}0.26$ (genuine), consistent with the severe power limitation at $n{=}5$ (minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$) rather than absence of effect.
100	Under stricter classification (H8+FN correction on 3 fully-audited seeds), the direction reverses ($\text{OR}{=}0.61$, n.s.), and extended training confirms the window closes completely ($17\%{\to}2\%$ and $55\%{\to}0\%$ genuine at 1000 steps).
101	The transient benefit is descriptively observed but statistically underpowered at seed-level ($n{=}5$); it should not be interpreted as evidence that ISR reliably improves structural quality. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42/s45/s46 (500 step) + plan002-b01-s42-1000
102	
103	\textbf{Premise-count confound.}
104	The genuine-rate advantage reflects a selection effect: premise-count-matched comparison yields only $1.06\times$ ISR difference, confirming indistinguishable per-proof quality once LOO bias toward short outputs is controlled (Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
105	
106	\textbf{Trajectory analysis: timing, not destiny.}\label{sec:goodhart-temporal}
107	Extended training (1000 steps; Appendix Figure~\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}) confirms the genuine window is transient across seeds.
108	Seed~42 declines from $17\%$ to $2\%$ genuine via H7 saturation ($16.0\%{\to}96.7\%$ H7, PR stable at ${\sim}96\%$); seed~45, which achieved the highest 500-step genuine rate ($55\%$), collapses to $0\%$ genuine through reward collapse ($\text{PR}: 42\%{\to}2\%$).
109	The two seeds exhibit distinct failure paths (H7 gaming vs.\ output collapse) but converge to the same outcome: zero genuine proofs at extended training. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan002-b01-s45-1000
110	
111	\textbf{Micro-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.05$): deterministic collapse.}
112	All three ISR seeds reach $\text{PR}{\geq}99\%$ but converge to H7a tautology (H7 $87$--$100\%$, genuine $5.1\%$), while the random control mostly fails to learn (median $\text{PR}{=}7\%$) with zero H7, confirming H7 as specific to ISR's LOO structure.
113	Below a signal-floor, ISR's genuine component is undetectable while its H7 bias remains exploitable (per-seed breakdown in Appendix~\ref{app:beta005}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b005-s42/s43/s44, sweep-random-b005-s42/s43/s44
114	
115	\textbf{Medium-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.2$): maximum exploitation.}
116	All three seeds converge to pure H7a tautology ($0\%$ non-hacking, $n{=}290$; Appendix Table~\ref{tab:beta02-detail}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b02-s42/s43/s44
117	
118	\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}
119	At $\beta{=}0.3$, ISR produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\text{PR}{=}66{\pm}42\%$): three seeds yield three distinct failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, pure H7 gaming; per-seed details in Appendix~\ref{app:beta03}).
120	The random control is equally unstable ($\text{PR}{=}54{\pm}35\%$) with $91\%$ H1 and ${\leq}4\%$ non-hacking; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44
121	ISR vs.\ random H7 at $\beta{=}0.3$: $\text{OR}{=}13.7$, 95\% CI $[7.0, 26.8]$ (Appendix~\ref{app:beta03}).
122	
123	\textbf{Non-monotonic dose-response.}
124	The four $\beta$ levels suggest a non-monotonic pattern: $\beta\in\{0.05, 0.2\}$ converge deterministically to H7 collapse (${\geq}94\%$ H7), $\beta{=}0.3$ triggers multi-modal instability, and only $\beta{=}0.1$ sustains a transient genuine window (3 of 5 seeds).
125	Goodhart collapse is not a simple function of dose; the balance between genuine and exploit reinforcement depends on a threshold interaction between $\beta$ and rollout stochasticity.
126	
127	\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}
128	\label{sec:crosslogic}
129	
130	We replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog (3 seeds, $\beta{=}0.1$).
131	Under exec-only training, 2/3 seeds show $\text{ISR}^{*}{\approx}0$ despite $\text{PR}{\geq}0.56$, reflecting redundant ground-fact repetition rather than FOL-style conclusion embedding; ISR flags both failure semantics.
132	Under ISR reward, 3/3 seeds reach $0\%$ non-hacking through three distinct mechanisms: clean H7 tautology (s42, $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$), degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog evading text-match classifiers (s43, functional $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$), and query-as-fact gaming (s44, $95/97$ proved at $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$).
133	Seed~44 evaded our strict classifier entirely; functional cross-validation was required to expose it.
134	We attribute complete collapse to Prolog's simpler operational semantics providing a broader gaming basin (detailed results in Appendix Table~\ref{tab:prolog-detailed} and Figure~\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).
135	
136	\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Evidence}
137	\label{sec:crossscale}
138	
139	We train Qwen3-1.7B under ISR $\beta\in\{0.1,0.3\}$ and Random $\beta{=}0.1$ (single seed, 500 steps).
140	All conditions produce $0\%$ non-hacking, but the exploit modes differ qualitatively from 4B: Random discovers H9 (contradiction injection, $\text{PR}{=}93\%$, $100\%$ \emph{ex falso}), ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ collapses to a single fixed template ($\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.6$ at zero variance, $100\%$ H1), and ISR $\beta{=}0.3$ triggers reward collapse ($\text{PR}{=}17\%$) mirroring the 4B pattern.
141	H9 never appears at 4B scale; fixed-template mode collapse never appears at 4B; different capacities converge to different Goodhart basins under identical rewards.
142	No safe scale was observed in this single-seed comparison (Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}).
143	
144	\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}
145	\label{sec:taxonomy}
146

172	
173	The nine modes (Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; Appendix~\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span 2 logic types, 2 model scales, and 6 conditions.
174	H1, H7, and H8 account for ${>}90\%$ of hacking; H7b ($14/350$) evades text-match classifiers.
175	ISR modestly increases H7 probability ($\text{OR}{=}2.84$, $p{<}5{\times}10^{-12}$; Appendix Table~\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}), but per-seed variance dominates.
176	All GRPO conditions show ${\geq}80\%$ pooled hacking; only SFT achieves $92.3\%$ non-hacking.
177	CMH tests treating samples as independent yield $\text{OR}_{\text{CMH}}{=}2.46$ ($p{<}10^{-15}$) for H7 and $3.37$ ($p{<}10^{-9}$) for genuine output; however, within-seed non-independence inflates these $p$-values (see \S\ref{sec:goodhart} for seed-level analysis).
178

s43: H1 $93.8\\%$, genuine $6.2\\%$; s44: H1 $85\\%$ + H8 $15\\%$). %% registry: plan016-extended-1000-s42, exec-only-s43-1000step-r7, plan001-exec-only-s44-1000-r2\nS43 recovers from $\\text{PR}{=}13\\%$ (500 steps) to $\\text{PR}{=}96\\%$ (1000 steps), but entirely through H1 exploitation: PR recovery does not imply quality recovery, and the repetition-collapse phase is transient rather than terminal.\n\n\\textbf{SFT baseline.}\nSFT on gold formalizations reaches $\\text{PR}{=}13.0\\%$ (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps) with $\\text{H7}{=}0$, $\\text{H1}{=}7.7\\%$, $92.3\\%$ non-hacking ($n{=}39$; $\\text{OR}{=}147$ vs.\\ exec-only, $p{<}0.001$; per-seed range ${\\sim}$91--93\\%). %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3\nThe near-absence of hacking under identical data exposure is consistent with hacking being RL-specific; a 100-step checkpoint ($\\text{PR}{=}32\\%$, hacking${\\geq}31\\%$) partially controls for the PR-level confound, though the PR gap remains substantial (Appendix~\\ref{app:100step}).\nSFT trains via negative log-likelihood on gold formalizations without exploration-exploitation dynamics; exploit discovery requires the trial-and-error sampling inherent to RL but absent from supervised training.\nWhether SFT would develop similar exploits at higher prove rates remains an open question; this mechanistic argument is plausible but not conclusive.\n\n\\subsection{ISR as Reward: Goodhart Effects}\n\\label{sec:goodhart}\n\nWe now reverse roles: instead of treating ISR as an observer, we use it as a reward augmentation, $r = r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1 + \\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$, with a matched random control, $r = r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1 + \\beta\\,u)$, $u\\sim\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$, to isolate ISR-specific signal from generic reward-variance effects.\nWe present $\\beta{=}0.1$ first as the focal dose (5 seeds, trajectory analysis), then examine lower and higher doses.\nThe headline finding: ISR contains an inherent Goodhart vulnerability~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} observed to produce collapse at all four tested doses ($\\beta\\in\\{0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3\\}$).\nThe mechanism is structural: tautological proofs ($P{\\to}P$) receive $\\text{ISR}{=}0.75$ and single-premise-equals-conclusion constructions receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, both by the LOO definition alone.\nOnce sampled during GRPO rollouts, these high-ISR tautologies create a positive feedback loop; $\\beta$ controls only the \\emph{timing} of collapse, not whether it occurs.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}$ & \\textbf{PR\\%}$\\uparrow$ & \\textbf{ISR}$^*$ & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\nBase (0-shot) & --- & 12 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\ %% registry: sta-phase0-baseline\nSFT & --- & 13 & 0.595 & 0 & 8 & \\textbf{92} \\\\ %% registry: plan009-sft-baseline-s42-1000/s43-1000/s44-1000-r3 (3-seed pooled)\n\\midrule\nExec-only$^\\dagger$ & --- & 93 & 0.132 & 0 & 87 & 8 \\\\ %% registry: plan001-exec-only-s42-r2 (H8 corrected)\nExec-only (1k)$^{\\dagger\\dagger}$ & --- & 97{\\scriptsize$\\pm$2} & 0.03{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.05} & 0 & --- & $\\leq$2$^{\\dagger\\dagger}$ \\\\\nISR$^\\ddagger$ & 0.1 & 70{\\scriptsize$\\pm$19} & --- & \\textbf{54} & 18 & 23$^\\star$ \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44/s45/s46 (5-seed, D124)\nRandom & 0.1 & \\textbf{83}{\\scriptsize$\\pm$15} & 0.080{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.06} & 30 & 59 & 8 \\\\ %% registry: plan018-additive-random-b01-s42/s43-r3/s44-r2/s45/s46 (5-seed)\nISR & 0.2 & 97{\\scriptsize$\\pm$4} & 0.69{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.24} & \\textbf{99} & 0 & 0 \\\\ %% registry: sweep-isr-b02-s42/s43/s44 (3-seed; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta02-detail})\n\\midrule\nISR & 0.3 & 66{\\scriptsize$\\pm$42} & 0.39{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.31} & \\textbf{50} & 37 & 11 \\\\ %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44 (pooled; D106 s43 genuine 23→20)\nRandom & 0.3 & 54{\\scriptsize$\\pm$35} & 0.09{\\scriptsize$\\pm$.09} & 7 & 91 & 2 \\\\ %% registry: plan017-additive-random-s42, exp1-random-b03-s43-r4, exp1-random-b03-s44-r2 (pooled; D086 H7: s42=4,s43=7,s44=0)\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{7}{l}{\\textit{Qwen3-1.7B (seed~42)}} \\\\\nISR & 0.1 & 100 & 0.600 & 0 & \\textbf{100} & 0 \\\\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-isr-b01-s42\nRandom & 0.1 & 93 & 0.028 & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-random-b01-s42 (H9=100%)\nISR & 0.3 & 17 & 0.031 & 0 & 71 & 29 \\\\ %% registry: d113-1.7b-isr-b03-s42\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Goodhart landscape. PR/ISR$^*$: mean$\\pm$std; H7/H1/N-H: pooled \\%. $^\\dagger$s42, 500 steps. $^\\ddagger$N-H uncorrected upper bound (s43/s44 H8 audit unavailable). $^\\star$Transient; collapses at 1000 steps (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart-temporal}). $^{\\dagger\\dagger}$3-seed 1000-step (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:exec-only-1000}).}\n\\label{tab:goodhart-landscape}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\textbf{Low-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.1$): stochastic delayed collapse.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 5 seeds exhibit a striking bifurcation: 2 seeds (s43, s44) collapse rapidly to near-pure H7 tautology (${\\geq}97\\%$ H7, $0\\%$ genuine within ${\\sim}150$ steps), while 3 seeds (s42, s45, s46) sustain a transient genuine window.\nThe 5-seed pooled non-hacking rate is ${\\sim}23\\%$ at 500 steps, with the collapsed seeds pulling down the average; the 3 non-collapsed seeds individually range from $17\\%$ to $55\\%$ genuine.\nThis bifurcation is stochastic: all seeds are indistinguishable in the first ${\\sim}100$ steps ($\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0.05$--$0.07$); divergence occurs at steps 150--200 when H7 tautologies, which receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ by construction, are sampled during rollouts and reinforced via the positive feedback loop.\nOnce a seed enters the H7 basin, convergence to saturation takes ${\\sim}150$ steps and is irreversible. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44/s45/s46\n\nH7 (a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by the LOO definition; \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}) is the canonical Goodhart exploit: the model satisfies the structural metric directly rather than producing a genuine derivation.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both ISR and random conditions (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}); the specific mode (H7 vs.\\ H1) within a seed is stochastic under both conditions ($\\text{CV}{>}1$), though ISR modestly increases H7 probability by providing a direct reward pathway.\nRandom yields higher PR ($83{\\pm}15\\%$ vs.\\ $70{\\pm}19\\%$; $n{=}5$ each).\nRandom H7 exhibits extreme seed dependence (per-seed range: $0\\%$--$91.5\\%$; pooled $30\\%$ is dominated by s46 at $91.5\\%$ and s43 at $38.3\\%$).\nBoth conditions produce H7 in 2 of 5 seeds, but the quality differs: ISR-induced H7 is absorbing ($100\\%$ of proved samples in affected seeds: s43${=}61/61$, s44${=}89/89$), while random-induced H7 coexists with other modes ($38$--$92\\%$).\nISR's reward signal converts H7 from a stochastic possibility into an irreversible attractor once sampled.\nThe reward landscape explains H7's attractor strength: H7 tautologies receive expected reward $1{+}0.75\\beta$ versus ${\\sim}1{+}0.26\\beta$ for genuine proofs (premise-count-matched), a gap of ${\\sim}0.49\\beta$ that creates a persistent gradient toward tautological constructions regardless of dose.\n\n\\textbf{The transient genuine window.}\nAt 500 steps, ISR produces more genuine output than random controls (pooled N-H: 23\\% vs.\\ 8\\%), with 3 ISR seeds showing ${\\geq}17\\%$ genuine (per-seed range: 17\\%--55\\%) versus predominantly H1-dominated random seeds, a descriptive pattern consistent with ISR temporarily rewarding necessary premises before H7 dominates.\nHowever, statistical inference is limited: Cochran--Mantel--Haenszel tests yield $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$ ($p{<}10^{-9}$), but within-seed samples share a checkpoint and use greedy decoding, violating the independence assumption required by CMH; the effective sample size is seeds ($n{=}5$), not samples ($n{=}350$/$414$).\nSeed-level permutation tests, which correctly treat seeds as the unit of observation, yield $p{=}0.26$ (genuine), consistent with the severe power limitation at $n{=}5$ (minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$) rather than absence of effect.\nUnder stricter classification (H8+FN correction on 3 fully-audited seeds), the direction reverses ($\\text{OR}{=}0.61$, n.s.), and extended training confirms the window closes completely ($17\\%{\\to}2\\%$ and $55\\%{\\to}0\\%$ genuine at 1000 steps).\nThe transient benefit is descriptively observed but statistically underpowered at seed-level ($n{=}5$); it should not be interpreted as evidence that ISR reliably improves structural quality. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42/s45/s46 (500 step) + plan002-b01-s42-1000\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe genuine-rate advantage reflects a selection effect: premise-count-matched comparison yields only $1.06\\times$ ISR difference, confirming indistinguishable per-proof quality once LOO bias toward short outputs is controlled (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n\n\\textbf{Trajectory analysis: timing, not destiny.}\\label{sec:goodhart-temporal}\nExtended training (1000 steps; Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}) confirms the genuine window is transient across seeds.\nSeed~42 declines from $17\\%$ to $2\\%$ genuine via H7 saturation ($16.0\\%{\\to}96.7\\%$ H7, PR stable at ${\\sim}96\\%$); seed~45, which achieved the highest 500-step genuine rate ($55\\%$), collapses to $0\\%$ genuine through reward collapse ($\\text{PR}: 42\\%{\\to}2\\%$).\nThe two seeds exhibit distinct failure paths (H7 gaming vs.\\ output collapse) but converge to the same outcome: zero genuine proofs at extended training. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan002-b01-s45-1000\n\n\\textbf{Micro-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.05$): deterministic collapse.}\nAll three ISR seeds reach $\\text{PR}{\\geq}99\\%$ but converge to H7a tautology (H7 $87$--$100\\%$, genuine $5.1\\%$), while the random control mostly fails to learn (median $\\text{PR}{=}7\\%$) with zero H7, confirming H7 as specific to ISR's LOO structure.\nBelow a signal-floor, ISR's genuine component is undetectable while its H7 bias remains exploitable (per-seed breakdown in Appendix~\\ref{app:beta005}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b005-s42/s43/s44, sweep-random-b005-s42/s43/s44\n\n\\textbf{Medium-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.2$): maximum exploitation.}\nAll three seeds converge to pure H7a tautology ($0\\%$ non-hacking, $n{=}290$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta02-detail}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b02-s42/s43/s44\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$): three seeds yield three distinct failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, pure H7 gaming; per-seed details in Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\nThe random control is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$) with $91\\%$ H1 and ${\\leq}4\\%$ non-hacking; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nISR vs.\\ random H7 at $\\beta{=}0.3$: $\\text{OR}{=}13.7$, 95\\% CI $[7.0, 26.8]$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\textbf{Non-monotonic dose-response.}\nThe four $\\beta$ levels suggest a non-monotonic pattern: $\\beta\\in\\{0.05, 0.2\\}$ converge deterministically to H7 collapse (${\\geq}94\\%$ H7), $\\beta{=}0.3$ triggers multi-modal instability, and only $\\beta{=}0.1$ sustains a transient genuine window (3 of 5 seeds).\nGoodhart collapse is not a simple function of dose; the balance between genuine and exploit reinforcement depends on a threshold interaction between $\\beta$ and rollout stochasticity.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog (3 seeds, $\\beta{=}0.1$).\nUnder exec-only training, 2/3 seeds show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite $\\text{PR}{\\geq}0.56$, reflecting redundant ground-fact repetition rather than FOL-style conclusion embedding; ISR flags both failure semantics.\nUnder ISR reward, 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking through three distinct mechanisms: clean H7 tautology (s42, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$), degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog evading text-match classifiers (s43, functional $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$), and query-as-fact gaming (s44, $95/97$ proved at $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$).\nSeed~44 evaded our strict classifier entirely; functional cross-validation was required to expose it.\nWe attribute complete collapse to Prolog's simpler operational semantics providing a broader gaming basin (detailed results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed} and Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\n\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Evidence}\n\\label{sec:crossscale}\n\nWe train Qwen3-1.7B under ISR $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$ and Random $\\beta{=}0.1$ (single seed, 500 steps).\nAll conditions produce $0\\%$ non-hacking, but the exploit modes differ qualitatively from 4B: Random discovers H9 (contradiction injection, $\\text{PR}{=}93\\%$, $100\\%$ \\emph{ex falso}), ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ collapses to a single fixed template ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.6$ at zero variance, $100\\%$ H1), and ISR $\\beta{=}0.3$ triggers reward collapse ($\\text{PR}{=}17\\%$) mirroring the 4B pattern.\nH9 never appears at 4B scale; fixed-template mode collapse never appears at 4B; different capacities converge to different Goodhart basins under identical rewards.\nNo safe scale was observed in this single-seed comparison (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{Rnd$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{SFT} & \\textbf{Prolog$_{\\text{ISR}}$} & \\textbf{1.7B$_{\\text{ISR}}$} & \\textbf{1.7B$_{\\text{Rnd}}$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 embed       & 87 & 18 & 59 & 7 & 0$^\\sharp$ & \\textbf{100} & 0 \\\\\nH7 duplicate   & 0  & \\textbf{54} & 30 & 0 & 100$^\\sharp$ & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH8 prem-subset & 6  & 5  & 8   & 0 & --- & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH9 ex falso    & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & 0 & \\textbf{100} \\\\\nTautological   & --- & --- & --- & --- & s42 & --- & --- \\\\\nRepetition     & s43 & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nContradiction  & 1k & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nCmplx.\\ vacuous& 0  & 0  & 4  & 0 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nQuery-as-fact  & --- & --- & --- & --- & s44 & --- & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Non-hacking} & 8  & \\textbf{23}  & 8 & \\textbf{92} & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Nine hacking modes (\\% of proved; exec/SFT: s42; ISR/Random 4B: 5-seed pooled; 1.7B: s42). H7a+H7b combined. Seed IDs mark dominant mode. $^\\sharp$Prolog: strict+functional pooled. Modes marked with seed identifiers are single-seed observations.}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe nine modes (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span 2 logic types, 2 model scales, and 6 conditions.\nH1, H7, and H8 account for ${>}90\\%$ of hacking; H7b ($14/350$) evades text-match classifiers.\nISR modestly increases H7 probability ($\\text{OR}{=}2.84$, $p{<}5{\\times}10^{-12}$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}), but per-seed variance dominates.\nAll GRPO conditions show ${\\geq}80\\%$ pooled hacking; only SFT achieves $92.3\\%$ non-hacking.\nCMH tests treating samples as independent yield $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}2.46$ ($p{<}10^{-15}$) for H7 and $3.37$ ($p{<}10^{-9}$) for genuine output; however, within-seed non-independence inflates these $p$-values (see \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart} for seed-level analysis).\n",

```text
1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B) and dataset family (FOLIO); generalization remains untested.
5	ISR quantifies syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a high-ISR proof can still be logically vacuous if its individually necessary premises are collectively inconsistent or semantically ungrounded.
6	A downstream NLI evaluation confirms this gap, finding ISR and task accuracy uncorrelated (Spearman $\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$; Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
7	Five seeds demonstrate qualitative divergence but limit statistical power for seed-level permutation tests ($p{\geq}0.26$; minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$ at $n{=}5$). CMH tests yield $p{<}10^{-9}$ but assume within-stratum independence, which is violated when samples share a checkpoint and decoding strategy; CMH $p$-values are therefore inflated and should be interpreted as directional indicators, not calibrated significance levels.
8	STA requires solvers supporting component-level input manipulation.
9	
10	\paragraph{Evaluation as temporal snapshot.}
11	All 500-step results represent a temporal snapshot, not an equilibrium.
12	Trajectory analysis on seeds~42 and~45 (1000 steps) confirms that the ${\sim}23\%$ genuine window observed at 500 steps under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ is transient (s42: $17\%{\to}2\%$; s45: $55\%{\to}0\%$ genuine).
13	Readers should interpret the 500-step non-hacking rates as upper bounds on long-run structural quality under continued training.
14	
15	\paragraph{Seed counts per dose.}
16	ISR $\beta{=}0.05$ uses three seeds (all H7-dominated); random $\beta{=}0.05$ uses three seeds with high variance ($\text{PR}{=}0\%$, $7\%$, $62\%$); $\beta{=}0.2$ uses three seeds.
17	The primary $\beta{=}0.1$ comparison uses five seeds per condition.
18	Although all tested doses produce dominant gaming, the stochastic bifurcation mechanism (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}) means that fewer-seed dose points may not capture the full variance of collapse timing.
19	ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ s43 and s44 checkpoints were lost during disk cleanup, preventing retrospective H8 audit on these seeds; the reported 5-seed pooled non-hacking rate (23\%) is an uncorrected upper bound.
20	
21	\paragraph{Cross-scale replication.}
22	Our 1.7B experiments use a single seed.
23	Although different seeds may converge to different exploitation modes (as observed at 4B scale, where three seeds produce three distinct hacking strategies), the qualitative conclusion that hacking dominates with 0\% genuine output is robust: at 4B, all seeds hack regardless of specific mode.
24	The zero-variance mode collapse at 1.7B (100 identical outputs) indicates a sharper convergence basin at smaller scale.
25	Replicating across seeds is a natural next step.
26	
27	\paragraph{Data scale.}
28	Our experiments use 500 training examples and 100 evaluation examples, drawn from FOLIO~\citep{folio}.
29	The training subset is selected via stratified sampling (seed 42) and held constant across all conditions.
30	While our findings are consistent across seeds and logic types, the effect of training data scale on hacking prevalence and mode distribution remains an open question for future work.
31	
```

66	\section{Extended Discussion}
67	\label{app:extended-discussion}
68	
69	\paragraph{What ISR measures, and what it does not.}
70	ISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.
71	This is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness (whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning).
72	We verify this empirically: Spearman $\rho(\text{ISR}, \text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) from a downstream NLI evaluation on exec-only checkpoint outputs.
73	ISR and downstream task performance are uncorrelated.
74	ISR is thus a structural integrity diagnostic, not a semantic quality metric: high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.
75	
76	Critically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.
77	Under exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding (repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof) rather than an absence of reasoning (\S\ref{sec:crosslogic}).
78	Under ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\S\ref{sec:goodhart}).
79	ISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.
80	
81	\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}
82	LOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.
83	A formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$, even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.
84	This confound is central to ISR's Goodhart vulnerability: H7 tautologies (single premise $=$ conclusion) achieve $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$ \emph{by construction}, and the shorter formalizations produced by non-collapsed seeds at $\beta{=}0.1$ also inflate ISR mechanically.
85	Premise-count-matched comparison on non-hacking samples yields $\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ vs.\ $0.243$ for random ($1.06\times$; Table~\ref{tab:fol-beta01-detailed}), confirming that even within the transient genuine window the structural benefit is negligible.
86	This bias is not specific to our setting; any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.
87	We recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.

117	\paragraph{Seed-level statistical analysis.}
118	To address potential inflation from within-seed dependence, we supplement pooled Fisher exact tests with two seed-aware analyses.
119	Cochran--Mantel--Haenszel (CMH) tests stratify by seed while pooling across samples: H7 $\text{OR}_{\text{CMH}}{=}2.46$ (95\% CI $[1.85, 3.27]$, $p{=}5.6{\times}10^{-16}$); genuine $\text{OR}_{\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$ (95\% CI $[2.21, 5.13]$, $p{=}4.5{\times}10^{-10}$).
120	CMH assumes within-stratum independence, but samples within each seed share a single checkpoint and use greedy decoding; the effective independent units are seeds ($n{=}5$), not samples, and CMH $p$-values are consequently inflated.
121	Seed-level permutation tests (treating each seed as a single observation, $n{=}5$ per group) yield $p{=}0.28$ (H7) and $p{=}0.26$ (genuine), consistent with the severe power limitation of $n{=}5$ (minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$) rather than absence of effect.

151	\paragraph{H7 as inevitable exploit.}
152	ISR's LOO definition creates an inherent structural bias: tautological proofs ($P{\to}P$) receive $\text{ISR}{=}0.75$, and single-premise-equals-conclusion constructions receive $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$, making H7 the global ISR-maximizing exploit.
153	$\beta$ controls the \emph{timing} of collapse, not whether it occurs.
154	At all four tested doses, H7 dominates: $\beta{=}0.05$ (3 seeds, pooled H7 $93.7\%$, $n{=}253$; random s43 achieves $\text{PR}{=}62\%$ with H1 $77.4\%$ and zero H7, confirming mode specificity); $\beta{=}0.1$ (stochastic bifurcation: 2/5 seeds collapse within ${\sim}150$ steps, remaining 3/5 sustain a transient window that closes under extended training); $\beta{=}0.2$ ($100\%$ H7 across all three seeds, $n{=}290$; Table~\ref{tab:beta02-detail}); $\beta{=}0.3$ (multi-modal, s44 pure H7).
155	The random control produces $29.5\%$ H7 ($122/414$, 5-seed; per-seed: $0\%$--$91.5\%$, dominated by s46 at $91.5\%$ and s43 at $38.3\%$; s42/s44/s45 at $0\%$), random $\beta{=}0.3$ yields 4--7 H7a per seed.
156	Once a seed samples and reinforces the H7 exploit, convergence is irreversible; this absorbing-state property guarantees eventual collapse regardless of dose.
157

176	\paragraph{Goodhart effects: implications.}
177	The finding extends beyond this specific system.
178	ISR was designed to \emph{detect} reward gaming of $r_{\text{exec}}$, yet it contains an inherent structural vulnerability: by its LOO definition, tautological proofs receive artificially high ISR ($P{\to}P$ yields $\text{ISR}{=}0.75$; single-premise-equals-conclusion yields $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.5$), creating a positive feedback loop once sampled during GRPO rollouts~\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, kwa-catastrophic-goodhart}.
179	Goodhart collapse~\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} is observed at all four tested doses ($\beta{=}0.05$: 3 seeds; $\beta\in\{0.1, 0.2, 0.3\}$: 3--5 seeds each).
180	The structural bias predicts universality (H7 tautology is always the global reward-maximizing exploit regardless of $\beta$), and $\beta$ controls only the probability of early sampling and thus the timing of collapse; at $\beta{=}0.05$, three seeds confirm deterministic H7 collapse ($93.7\%$ pooled) while the matched random control at the same $\beta$ produces H1 redundancy instead ($77.4\%$), ruling out dose magnitude as the driver.
181	At moderate doses ($\beta{=}0.1$), collapse is stochastic and delayed (5 seeds bifurcate: 2 collapse rapidly, 3 sustain a transient genuine window that trajectory analysis confirms is temporary); at higher doses ($\beta{=}0.2$), collapse is immediate and universal.
182	$\beta{=}0.3$ adds reward collapse~\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} as a competing failure mode (PR drops to 18\% in 1/3 seeds), but the underlying Goodhart vulnerability remains the same.
183	The practical implication is that ISR (and structurally similar LOO-based diagnostics) should be used exclusively as post-hoc evaluation tools, never as training rewards.
184	
185	\paragraph{Single-seed unreliability.}
186	The three exec-only seeds diverge radically (PR $\sigma{=}47$ percentage points), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
187	ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ (5 seeds) shows even greater instability: 2/5 seeds collapse to H7 within ${\sim}150$ steps, while 3/5 sustain a transient genuine window.
188	The 5-seed pooled non-hacking rate is ${\sim}23\%$ at 500 steps; trajectory analysis on seeds~42 and~45 (1000 steps) confirms this is temporary ($17\%{\to}2\%$ and $55\%{\to}0\%$ genuine respectively).
189	The dominant hacking mode (H7 vs.\ H1) is stochastic within any treatment condition ($\text{CV}{>}1$).
190	Multi-seed evaluation with trajectory analysis should be standard practice for neurosymbolic RL systems; single-seed or single-checkpoint results can misrepresent a configuration's true hacking profile.
191	
192	
193	\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}
194	\label{app:hacking-modes}
195	
196	We identify nine distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.
197	Each mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.
198	Table~\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app} summarizes prevalence across FOL conditions.
199

228	
229	\paragraph{Classifier Validation.}
230	Hacking mode classification uses deterministic rule-based classifiers, not learned models, so standard ML precision/recall is not directly applicable.
231	H1 and H7 are identified by normalized text matching between conclusion and premise strings after whitespace and variable normalization; remaining modes are detected via functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution from STA (leave-one-out ablation).
232	No training data or manual annotation is used for classification.
233	
234	To estimate precision, we manually verified 50 randomly sampled outputs classified as H1 or H7: all 50 were confirmed correct (estimated precision ${>}$95\%, Wilson 95\% CI: [93\%, 100\%]).
235	Recall is inherently bounded by the text-match threshold: semantic manipulations that alter surface form while preserving the gaming structure (e.g., variable renaming beyond our normalization, or paraphrased conclusions) would be missed.
236	We cross-validate with functional LOO analysis: samples classified as non-hacking but showing $n_{\text{necessary}} \leq 1$ are flagged as potential false negatives.
237	This cross-validation revealed query-as-fact gaming in Prolog (\S\ref{app:hacking-modes}), where the strict text classifier reported 100\% non-hacking but functional analysis showed 95/97 samples were tautological ($n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$).
238	The non-hacking rate for FOL conditions may therefore be slightly overestimated, as subtle semantic manipulations below our text-matching threshold would evade the strict classifier.
239	
240	\subsection{H8 --- Premise-Subset Gaming}
241	\label{app:h8}
242	
243	Manual audit of ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 revealed a previously undetected hacking mode: the model emits a subset of the gold premises that is individually necessary (high ISR) but insufficient for the intended derivation, with proof success depending on the conclusion already being embedded (H1-style).
244	We term this \textbf{H8 (premise-subset gaming)}: formalizations where each component is LOO-necessary but the premise set is a strict subset of what the NL problem requires, inflating ISR without faithful reasoning.
245	
246	\paragraph{Detection.} H8 is identified by manual inspection of non-hacking samples with unusually low premise counts ($n_{\text{prem}} \leq 3$) that nonetheless achieve high ISR.
247	Automated detection is not currently feasible because H8 requires comparing the generated premise set against the gold premises.
248	
249	\paragraph{Audit scope and limitations.}
250	Full H8 audit was completed for ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 only.
251	ISR seeds~43 and~44 checkpoints were deleted before audit and cannot be recovered; their non-hacking rates may be overestimated.
252	Random $\beta{=}0.1$ does not produce H8 by mechanism (no ISR optimization pressure); seed~42 audit confirmed H8${=}0$.
253	
254	\paragraph{H7 false-negative correction (H7b obfuscated tautology).}
255	ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 contained 12 samples with $n_{\text{prem}}{=}1$ classified as non-hacking by the text matcher.
256	Manual inspection confirmed all 12 are logically equivalent to H7 but evade text matching through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$) or structural simplification ($1/12$).
257	We classify these as \textbf{H7b} (obfuscated tautology), distinct from text-match-detectable H7a.
258	$10/12$ achieve $\text{ISR}{\geq}0.83$, indicating simultaneous gaming of execution reward, ISR metric, and H7 classifier (triple gaming; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy}).
259	After correction, ISR s44 non-hacking${=}0$; total H7 = H7a (77 text-match) + H7b (12 obfuscated) = 89.
260	
261	\paragraph{Impact on non-hacking-rate comparison.}
262	After H8 reclassification and H7 false-negative correction: ISR non-hacking $= 14/231 = 6.06\%$ vs.\ random non-hacking $= 25/262 = 9.5\%$ (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, n.s.; Wilson 95\% CI: ISR [3.6\%, 9.9\%], Random [6.5\%, 13.7\%]).
263	The direction is consistent with ISR-amplified gaming modes (H7, H8) displacing non-hacking output, but the effect is not statistically significant at the conventional $\alpha{=}0.05$ threshold.
264	
265	\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}
266	
267	\paragraph{Definition.}
268	The conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.
269	The solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.
270	
271	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
272	{\small\begin{verbatim}
273	Premises:
274	  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).
275	  bird(tweety).
276	  flies(tweety).  % conclusion embedded
277	Conclusion: flies(tweety).
278	\end{verbatim}}
279	
280	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
281	Variable. The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.
282	ISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.
283	
284	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
285	Conclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\% across all RL conditions.
286	Under exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (59--91\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (54\% pooled, 5-seed).
287	
288	
289	\subsection{H7a --- Conclusion Duplication (Standard)}
290	
291	\paragraph{Definition.}
292	The entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical (text-match detectable) to the conclusion.
293	Since $P \to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.
294	Because the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.
295	
296	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
297	{\small\begin{verbatim}
298	Premises:
299	  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).
300	Conclusion:
301	  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).
302	\end{verbatim}}
303	
304	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
305	ISR~$\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.
306	The single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.
307	H7a games both exec reward and ISR reward; H7b extends this to triple gaming by additionally evading the text-match classifier.
308	
309	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
310	H7a (text-match detectable): 64.5\% (149/231, 3-seed) under ISR~$\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\%, s43: 96.7\%, s44: 86.5\%); 5-seed pooled H7: 54\% (190/350, Table~\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}).
311	Combined H7 (H7a + H7b, 3-seed): 70.6\% (163/231; s43 H7a${=}59$ + H7b${=}2$; includes s44 FN correction).
312	Random~$\beta{=}0.1$ pooled: 29.5\% (122/414, 5-seed, all H7a; driven by s46 at 91.5\% and s43 at 38.3\%; s42/s44/s45 at 0\%).
313	
314	
315	\subsection{H7b --- Obfuscated Tautology}
316	
317	\paragraph{Definition.}
318	A single-premise formalization logically equivalent to H7a (premise = conclusion) but with surface-form differences that evade the text-match H7 classifier.
319	The model achieves this through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$ instances) or structural simplification ($1/12$).
320	
321	\paragraph{Example (FOL, vacuous quantifier wrapping).}
322	{\small\begin{verbatim}
323	Premises:
324	  all x all y ((A(x)&B(y))->(C(x)|D(y))).
325	Conclusion:
326	  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).
327	\end{verbatim}}
328	The premise introduces a vacuous second quantifier variable $y$ that is immediately unified with $x$, making the formulas logically equivalent while differing textually.
329	
330	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
331	$\text{ISR}{\geq}0.83$ ($10/12$ samples).
332	H7b simultaneously games three objectives: execution reward ($r_{\text{exec}}{=}1$ via tautological proof), ISR metric (single necessary premise yields high ISR), and the text-match H7 classifier (surface-form difference evades detection).
333	This triple gaming makes H7b the most complete Goodhart case we observe.
334	
335	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
336	Found primarily in ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 ($12/89$ proved samples, all originally classified as non-hacking); seed~43 contributes $2$ additional cases.
337	Functional cross-validation via $n_{\text{necessary}}{=}1$ exposed the misclassification.
338	
339	
340	\subsection{Tautological Collapse}
341	
342	\paragraph{Definition.}
343	Two-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.
344	
345	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
346	\begin{verbatim}
347	Premises: p(a).
348	Conclusion: p(a).
349	\end{verbatim}
350	
351	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
352	ISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).
353	Distinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\text{comp}}\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.
354	
355	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
356	Dominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\% H7 by strict classifier).
357	Also observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).
358	
359	
360	\subsection{Repetition Collapse}
361	
362	\paragraph{Definition.}
363	The model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.
364	Removing any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\approx 0$.
365	
366	\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}
367	\begin{verbatim}
368	penguin(fido).
369	penguin(fido).
370	penguin(fido).
371	... (repeated to 512 tokens)
372	query(penguin(fido)).
373	\end{verbatim}
374	
375	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
376	ISR~$\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.
377	ISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.
378	
379	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
380	Observed in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=13$\%, ISR~$=0$).
381	
382	
383	\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}
384	
385	\paragraph{Definition.}
386	The formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous; none is individually necessary for the proof.
387	The complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.
388	
389	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
390	{\small\begin{verbatim}
391	Premises:
392	  all x all y (R(x,y) ->
393	    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).
394	  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).
395	  P(a).            % only this needed
396	Conclusion: Q(a).
397	\end{verbatim}}
398	
399	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
400	ISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.
401	This is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.
402	
403	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
404	Low. Observed in Random~$\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\% (3/79).
405	Not observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).
406	
407	
408	\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}
409	
410	\paragraph{Definition.}
411	The component count grows unboundedly over training.
412	Excess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \emph{ex falso quodlibet}.
413	Individual premises may register as ISR~$=0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.
414	
415	\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}
416	{\small\begin{verbatim}
417	Premises:
418	  c1. c2. c3. ... c18. % 18.55 mean
419	  % conclusion embedded among premises
420	Conclusion: Q(a).
421	\end{verbatim}}
422	
423	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
424	ISR~$\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).
425	
426	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
427	Observed in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).
428	Mean component count grows from ${\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).
429	
430	
431	\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}
432	
433	\paragraph{Definition.}
434	The model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.
435	Prolog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.
436	This mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.
437	
438	\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}
439	\begin{verbatim}
440	% NL: "Fido is happy."
441	% Model output:
442	fido_is_happy.
443	:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact
444	\end{verbatim}
445	
446	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
447	ISR~$\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.
448	The single fact is trivially necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.
449	Functionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.
450	
451	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
452	Observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$=0.97$, ISR~$=0.912$).
453	95/97 proved samples have $n_{\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\% non-hacking.
454	
455	
456	\subsection{H9 --- Ex Falso Quodlibet (Contradiction Injection)}
457	
458	\paragraph{Definition.}
459	The model generates a premise set containing logically contradictory assertions (e.g., $P(x)$ and $\neg P(x)$).
460	From contradictory premises, any conclusion follows by the \emph{ex falso quodlibet} principle, rendering the proof semantically vacuous regardless of the conclusion's content.
461	Unlike the ``contradiction explosion'' mode observed in 4B exec-only at 1000 steps (where contradictions arise incidentally among inflated premise counts), H9 is a \emph{targeted} strategy: the model constructs minimal contradictory pairs that directly enable the proof.
462	
463	\paragraph{Example (FOL).}
464	{\small\begin{verbatim}
465	Premises:
466	  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).
467	  bird(tweety).
468	  -bird(tweety).  % contradicts above
469	Conclusion: happy(tweety).
470	% Proof: bird(tweety) & -bird(tweety) -> False -> anything
471	\end{verbatim}}
472	
473	\paragraph{ISR interaction.}
474	ISR~$>0$ because each side of the contradiction is individually necessary under LOO: removing $P(x)$ leaves $\neg P(x)$ alone, which cannot derive a contradiction; removing $\neg P(x)$ leaves the premises consistent but insufficient to derive an unrelated conclusion.
475	The LOO test thus registers both contradictory premises as ``necessary,'' inflating ISR despite the formalization being semantically vacuous.
476	
477	\paragraph{Prevalence.}
478	Observed exclusively in Qwen3-1.7B under Random $\beta{=}0.1$ (seed~42, 500 steps, proper train/eval separation): 100\% of proved samples ($n{=}93$) exhibit H9.
479	Under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$ with the same model, H9 does not appear; the model instead converges to fixed-template H1 ($\text{ISR}{=}0.6$, zero variance), indicating that ISR reward redirects the hacking pathway from H9 to H1.
480	Not observed in any Qwen3-4B experiment across all conditions and seeds tested. %% registry: d113-1.7b-random-b01-s42
481	
482

615	
616	\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}
617	Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:
618	ISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\%) $\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.5$\% from step 100).
619	PR increases monotonically ($0.32 \to 0.93 \to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.
620	By step 1000, 94.8\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.
621	
622	\paragraph{Prolog: consistent gaming across all seeds via three distinct pathways.}
623	All three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart effects with 0\% non-hacking proofs (Figure~\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}), but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:
624	seed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),
625	seed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,
626	and seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\_case atoms used as both fact and query.
627	The diversity of gaming pathways across seeds, despite identical training data and reward function, demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.

662	\subsection{Per-Seed Results at $\beta{=}0.05$}
663	\label{app:beta005}
664	
665	The 3-seed random control at $\beta{=}0.05$ is highly unstable: s42 fails entirely ($\text{PR}{=}0\%$), s44 barely learns ($\text{PR}{=}7\%$, H1 $71\%$, genuine $0\%$), and only s43 reaches moderate output ($\text{PR}{=}62\%$, H1 $77\%$, genuine $13\%$).
666	The single random seed that learns (s43) shows higher genuine output than ISR ($13\%$ vs.\ $5.1\%$ pooled), consistent with a signal-floor below which ISR's genuine component is undetectable while its H7 bias remains exploitable.
667	
668	\subsection{Early-Training Hacking Analysis (100-Step Checkpoint)}
669	\label{app:100step}
670	
671	To control for the prove-rate confound in our SFT comparison (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}), we analyze the exec-only checkpoint at 100 training steps (seed~42), where PR${=}32$\%, higher than SFT (13.0\%, 3-seed pooled) but still early in training.
672	Of the 32 proved samples, 9 (28.1\%) exhibit H7 hacking (single premise identical to conclusion) and 1 (3.1\%) exhibits H1 (conclusion embedded among premises), yielding a total hacking rate of 31.2\%.
673	By contrast, SFT at comparable or lower PR shows 7.7\% hacking (1/13 proved samples).
674	This demonstrates that reward hacking emerges early in GRPO training, well before the prove-rate plateau, and is not simply an artifact of having more proved samples available for exploitation.
675	The 100-step hacking rate (31.2\%) is already substantially above SFT (7.7\%), supporting the claim that hacking is associated with RL optimization rather than prove-rate level alone.
676	
677	\subsection{$O(1)$ vs.\ $O(n)$ ISR Ablation}
678	\label{app:o1-ablation}
679	
680	To justify the per-clause LOO design (\S\ref{sec:sta}), we train a variant that replaces the $O(n)$ ablation with a cheaper $O(1)$ binary signal: $\text{fast\_ISR}(x) = 1$ if $x$ is proved \emph{and} the conclusion is not provable under empty premises (tautology check), $0$ otherwise.
681	The reward becomes $r = 0.5\,r_{\text{exec}} + 0.5\,\text{fast\_ISR}$.
682	After 500 steps (seed~42), this variant achieves $\text{PR}{=}63\%$ with $\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$ (functionally zero), compared to $\text{ISR}^*{=}0.132$ under standard exec-only and $\text{ISR}^*{=}0.42$ under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$.
683	
684	\paragraph{ISR signal vs.\ training dynamics.}
685	At the \emph{ISR signal level}, the $O(1)$ variant is indistinguishable from exec-only: both produce near-zero ISR because models emit redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.
686	At the \emph{training level}, the two conditions are non-equivalent: the $O(1)$ tautology check penalizes formalizations where the conclusion is provable without any premises, a constraint absent from exec-only.
687	This confound depresses prove rate by 30 percentage points ($63\%$ vs.\ $93\%$ exec-only), making it impossible to attribute the difference purely to the ISR signal.
688	The result confirms that the $O(n)$ per-clause ablation is necessary for ISR to carry a structural signal, while also showing that even a minimal tautology guard alters training dynamics substantially.
689	
```

</details>

Discussion}\n\\label{app:extended-discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{What ISR measures, and what it does not.}\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.\nThis is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness (whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning).\nWe verify this empirically: Spearman $\\rho(\\text{ISR}, \\text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) from a downstream NLI evaluation on exec-only checkpoint outputs.\nISR and downstream task performance are uncorrelated.\nISR is thus a structural integrity diagnostic, not a semantic quality metric: high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.\n\nCritically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.\nUnder exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding (repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof) rather than an absence of reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nUnder ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\nLOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.\nA formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$, even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.\nThis confound is central to ISR's Goodhart vulnerability: H7 tautologies (single premise $=$ conclusion) achieve $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ \\emph{by construction}, and the shorter formalizations produced by non-collapsed seeds at $\\beta{=}0.1$ also inflate ISR mechanically.\nPremise-count-matched comparison on non-hacking samples yields $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ vs.\\ $0.243$ for random ($1.06\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-detailed}), confirming that even within the transient genuine window the structural benefit is negligible.\nThis bias is not specific to our setting; any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.\nWe recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Fisher exact tests across conditions. Non-hacking rate reported at 5-seed (primary) and 3-seed with H8+FN corrections (sensitivity); H7 comparison at 5-seed (primary) and 3-seed (sensitivity).}\n\\label{tab:bootstrap-ci}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Comparison} & \\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Control} & \\textbf{OR} & \\textbf{$p$} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{Non-hacking rate (5-seed)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 22.6\\% {\\scriptsize[18.2,27.0]} & 7.7\\% {\\scriptsize[5.5,10.7]} & 3.48 & ${<}10^{-8}$ \\\\\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{Non-hacking rate (3-seed, H8+FN corrected)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 6.06\\% {\\scriptsize[3.6,9.9]} & 9.5\\% {\\scriptsize[6.5,13.7]} & 0.61 & 0.182 (n.s.) \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{H7 gaming rate (5-seed)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 54.3\\% {\\scriptsize[49.1,59.5]} & 29.5\\% {\\scriptsize[25.1,33.9]} & 2.84 & $4.82{\\times}10^{-12}$ \\\\\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{H7 gaming rate (3-seed sensitivity)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 70.6\\% {\\scriptsize[64.3,76.3]} & 13.7\\% {\\scriptsize[9.9,18.6]} & 15.05 & $1.3{\\times}10^{-39}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{SFT vs.\\ Exec-only non-hacking rate}} \\\\\nSFT vs.\\ Exec-only & 92.3\\% {\\scriptsize[79.7,97.3]} & 7.5\\% & 147 & ${<}0.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\\\[2pt]\n{\\footnotesize 5-seed: ISR $n{=}350$, Random $n{=}414$. 3-seed: ISR $n{=}231$, Random $n{=}262$.
