the 3 non-collapsed seeds individually range from $17\\%$ to $55\\%$ genuine.\nThis bifurcation is stochastic: all seeds are indistinguishable in the first ${\\sim}100$ steps ($\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0.05$--$0.07$); divergence occurs at steps 150--200 when H7 tautologies, which receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ by construction, are sampled during rollouts and reinforced via the positive feedback loop.\nOnce a seed enters the H7 basin, convergence to saturation takes ${\\sim}150$ steps and is irreversible. %% registry: plan002-additive-b01-s42/s43-r2/s44/s45/s46\n\nH7 (a single premise identical to the conclusion, giving $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ by the LOO definition; \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}) is the canonical Goodhart exploit: the model satisfies the structural metric directly rather than producing a genuine derivation.\nTotal conclusion embedding ($\\text{H1}+\\text{H7}$) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\%$ across both ISR and random conditions (Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:hacking-composition}); the specific mode (H7 vs.\\ H1) within a seed is stochastic under both conditions ($\\text{CV}{>}1$), though ISR modestly increases H7 probability by providing a direct reward pathway.\nRandom yields higher PR ($83{\\pm}15\\%$ vs.\\ $70{\\pm}19\\%$; $n{=}5$ each).\nRandom H7 exhibits extreme seed dependence (per-seed range: $0\\%$--$91.5\\%$; pooled $30\\%$ is dominated by s46 at $91.5\\%$ and s43 at $38.3\\%$).\nBoth conditions produce H7 in 2 of 5 seeds, but the quality differs: ISR-induced H7 is absorbing ($100\\%$ of proved samples in affected seeds: s43${=}61/61$, s44${=}89/89$), while random-induced H7 coexists with other modes ($38$--$92\\%$).\nISR's reward signal converts H7 from a stochastic possibility into an irreversible attractor once sampled.\nThe reward landscape explains H7's attractor strength: H7 tautologies receive expected reward $1{+}0.75\\beta$ versus ${\\sim}1{+}0.26\\beta$ for genuine proofs (premise-count-matched), a gap of ${\\sim}0.49\\beta$ that creates a persistent gradient toward tautological constructions regardless of dose.\n\n\\textbf{The transient genuine window.}\nAt 500 steps, ISR produces more genuine output than random controls (pooled N-H: 23\\% vs.\\ 8\\%), with 3 ISR seeds showing ${\\geq}17\\%$ genuine (per-seed range: 17\\%--55\\%) versus predominantly H1-dominated random seeds, a descriptive pattern consistent with ISR temporarily rewarding necessary premises before H7 dominates.\nPooled Cochran--Mantel--Haenszel tests favor ISR ($\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$) but violate within-seed independence (greedy decoding from a shared checkpoint); seed-level permutation tests ($p{=}0.26$, $n{=}5$) are correctly specified but severely underpowered (minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$; Appendix~\\ref{app:statistical-details}).\nUnder stricter classification (H8+FN correction on 3 fully-audited seeds), the direction reverses ($\\text{OR}{=}0.61$, n.s.), and extended training confirms the window closes completely ($17\\%{\\to}2\\%$ and $55\\%{\\to}0\\%$ genuine at 1000 steps).\nThe transient benefit is descriptively observed but statistically underpowered at seed-level ($n{=}5$); it should not be interpreted as evidence that ISR reliably improves structural quality. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42/s45/s46 (500 step) + plan002-b01-s42-1000\n\n\\textbf{Premise-count confound.}\nThe genuine-rate advantage reflects a selection effect: premise-count-matched comparison yields only $1.06\\times$ ISR difference, confirming indistinguishable per-proof quality once LOO bias toward short outputs is controlled (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\n\n\\textbf{Trajectory analysis: timing, not destiny.}\\label{sec:goodhart-temporal}\nExtended training (1000 steps; Appendix Figure~\\ref{fig:training-isr-curves}) confirms the genuine window is transient across seeds.\nSeed~42 declines from $17\\%$ to $2\\%$ genuine via H7 saturation ($16.0\\%{\\to}96.7\\%$ H7, PR stable at ${\\sim}96\\%$); seed~45, which achieved the highest 500-step genuine rate ($55\\%$), collapses to $0\\%$ genuine through reward collapse ($\\text{PR}: 42\\%{\\to}2\\%$).\nThe two seeds exhibit distinct failure paths (H7 gaming vs.\\ output collapse) but converge to the same outcome: zero genuine proofs at extended training. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42, plan002-b01-s45-1000\n\n\\textbf{Micro-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.05$): deterministic collapse.}\nAll three ISR seeds reach $\\text{PR}{\\geq}99\\%$ but converge to H7a tautology (H7 $87$--$100\\%$, genuine $5.1\\%$), while the random control mostly fails to learn (median $\\text{PR}{=}7\\%$) with zero H7, confirming H7 as specific to ISR's LOO structure.\nBelow a signal-floor, ISR's genuine component is undetectable while its H7 bias remains exploitable (per-seed breakdown in Appendix~\\ref{app:beta005}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b005-s42/s43/s44, sweep-random-b005-s42/s43/s44\n\n\\textbf{Medium-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.2$): maximum exploitation.}\nAll three seeds converge to pure H7a tautology ($0\\%$ non-hacking, $n{=}290$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:beta02-detail}). %% registry: sweep-isr-b02-s42/s43/s44\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$): three seeds yield three distinct failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, pure H7 gaming; per-seed details in Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\nThe random control is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$) with $91\\%$ H1 and ${\\leq}4\\%$ non-hacking; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source. %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nISR vs.\\ random H7 at $\\beta{=}0.3$: $\\text{OR}{=}13.7$, 95\\% CI $[7.0, 26.8]$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\textbf{Non-monotonic dose-response.}\nThe four $\\beta$ levels suggest a non-monotonic pattern: $\\beta\\in\\{0.05, 0.2\\}$ converge deterministically to H7 collapse (${\\geq}94\\%$ H7), $\\beta{=}0.3$ triggers multi-modal instability, and only $\\beta{=}0.1$ sustains a transient genuine window (3 of 5 seeds).\nGoodhart collapse is not a simple function of dose; the balance between genuine and exploit reinforcement depends on a threshold interaction between $\\beta$ and rollout stochasticity.\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog (3 seeds, $\\beta{=}0.1$).\nUnder exec-only training, 2/3 seeds show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite $\\text{PR}{\\geq}0.56$, reflecting redundant ground-fact repetition rather than FOL-style conclusion embedding; ISR flags both failure semantics.\nUnder ISR reward, 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking through three distinct mechanisms: clean H7 tautology (s42, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$), degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog evading text-match classifiers (s43, functional $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$), and query-as-fact gaming (s44, $95/97$ proved at $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$).\nSeed~44 evaded our strict classifier entirely; functional cross-validation was required to expose it.\nWe attribute complete collapse to Prolog's simpler operational semantics providing a broader gaming basin (detailed results in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed} and Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\n\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Evidence}\n\\label{sec:crossscale}\n\nWe train Qwen3-1.7B under ISR $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$ and Random $\\beta{=}0.1$ (single seed, 500 steps).\nAll conditions produce $0\\%$ non-hacking, but the exploit modes differ qualitatively from 4B: Random discovers H9 (contradiction injection, $\\text{PR}{=}93\\%$, $100\\%$ \\emph{ex falso}), ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ collapses to a single fixed template ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.6$ at zero variance, $100\\%$ H1), and ISR $\\beta{=}0.3$ triggers reward collapse ($\\text{PR}{=}17\\%$) mirroring the 4B pattern.\nH9 never appears at 4B scale; fixed-template mode collapse never appears at 4B; different capacities converge to different Goodhart basins under identical rewards.\nNo safe scale was observed in this single-seed comparison (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}).\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{Rnd$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{SFT} & \\textbf{Prolog$_{\\text{ISR}}$} & \\textbf{1.7B$_{\\text{ISR}}$} & \\textbf{1.7B$_{\\text{Rnd}}$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 embed       & 87 & 18 & 59 & 7 & 0$^\\sharp$ & \\textbf{100} & 0 \\\\\nH7 duplicate   & 0  & \\textbf{54} & 30 & 0 & 100$^\\sharp$ & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH8 prem-subset & 6  & 5  & 8   & 0 & --- & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH9 ex falso    & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & 0 & \\textbf{100} \\\\\nTautological   & --- & --- & --- & --- & s42 & --- & --- \\\\\nRepetition     & s43 & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nContradiction  & 1k & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nCmplx.\\ vacuous& 0  & 0  & 4  & 0 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nQuery-as-fact  & --- & --- & --- & --- & s44 & --- & --- \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Non-hacking} & 8  & \\textbf{23}  & 8 & \\textbf{92} & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Nine hacking modes (\\% of proved; exec/SFT: s42; ISR/Random 4B: 5-seed pooled; 1.7B: s42). Modes marked with seed identifiers are single-seed observations.}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe nine modes (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span 2 logic types, 2 model scales, and 6 conditions.\nH1, H7, and H8 account for ${>}90\\%$ of hacking; H7b ($14/350$) evades text-match classifiers.\nISR modestly increases H7 probability ($\\text{OR}{=}2.84$, $p{<}5{\\times}10^{-12}$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}), but per-seed variance dominates.\nAll GRPO conditions show ${\\geq}80\\%$ pooled hacking; only SFT achieves $92.3\\%$ non-hacking.\nCMH tests treating samples as independent yield $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}2.46$ ($p{<}10^{-15}$) for H7 and $3.37$ ($p{<}10^{-9}$) for genuine output; however, within-seed non-independence inflates these $p$-values (see \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart} for seed-level analysis).\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nGRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on first-order logic problems ($97{\\pm}2\\%$ across three seeds, 1000 steps), yet at convergence produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs.\nEach seed develops a distinct degenerate shortcut: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, or tautological collapse, all invisible to execution-based evaluation, which registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}\nProve rate, the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}, conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.\nRecent work applies GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but provides no signal on structural quality.\nProcess reward models~\\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.\nA separate line of work documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \\emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.\n\n\\paragraph{Solver Trace Ablation.}\nWe address this gap with \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), which applies \\emph{component necessity testing} (leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs) to quantify structural integrity (Figure~\\ref{fig:sta-overview}).\nFor each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \\emph{Irreducible Solver Rate} (ISR), with $\\text{ISR}{=}1$ indicating full necessity and $\\text{ISR}{=}0$ complete redundancy.\nLOO ablation itself is standard~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; our contribution lies in applying it as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and in the empirical phenomena it surfaces.\nISR captures structural rather than semantic integrity (downstream NLI: $\\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$).\nAs a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\\citep{folio}, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR: $\\text{ISR}^{*}{:}\\,0.642\\to 0.132\\to 0.003$ over 100--1000 steps (seed~42; 99.5\\% degradation) while PR rises $32\\%{\\to}96\\%$.\nThree seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}47$\\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different mode: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse.\nAn SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\\text{PR}{=}13.0\\%$ with 92.3\\% non-hacking (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps), consistent with the failure being RL-specific rather than data- or capacity-driven.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR as reward.}\nThat ISR will be gamed is predictable from its definition (\\S\\ref{sec:method}); we characterize how collapse unfolds empirically.\nRepurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\\text{exec}} \\cdot (1 + \\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$, ISR contains an inherent structural vulnerability: tautological proofs (a single premise identical to the conclusion) receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ by construction, and implication tautologies ($P{\\to}P$) receive $\\text{ISR}{=}0.75$, creating a positive feedback loop once sampled during GRPO rollouts~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, kwa-catastrophic-goodhart}.\nGoodhart collapse~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} is observed at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\\beta{=}0.1$): $\\beta$ controls \\emph{when} collapse occurs, not \\emph{whether}.\nAt moderate doses, collapse is stochastic and delayed: seeds bifurcate between rapid convergence to tautology and a transient genuine window that closes under extended training.\nSnapshot evaluation would overestimate ISR's benefit; trajectory analysis confirms the window is temporary.\nThe effect transfers to Prolog (3/3 seeds yield 0\\% non-hacking via distinct mechanisms, including \\emph{query-as-fact gaming}) and is preliminarily observed at 1.7B scale (qualitatively novel gaming modes in single-seed evaluation).\nISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \\emph{reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer at any dose.}\n\n\\paragraph{Contributions.}\nAcross 6 reward conditions, 5 seeds, 2 logic types, 2 model scales, and up to 1000 steps, we contribute: (\\textbf{C1}) We empirically demonstrate ISR as a diagnostic for GRPO reward hacking, revealing a 99.5\\% collapse trajectory invisible to PR, with an SFT control consistent with RL-specific failure (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\\textbf{C2}) a characterization of Goodhart collapse dynamics under LOO-based rewards: stochastic bifurcation between collapse and genuine windows, non-monotonic dose-response across four $\\beta$ levels, and cross-logic mode divergence (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}--\\ref{sec:crosslogic}); (\\textbf{C3}) cross-logic transfer to Prolog with consistent Goodhart effects across all seeds (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}); (\\textbf{C4}) a nine-mode hacking taxonomy including query-as-fact gaming, obfuscated tautology, and a scale-specific \\emph{ex falso} mode, motivating functional cross-validation of diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\nThese are cautionary results: snapshot evaluation of structural rewards can overestimate their benefit; only trajectory analysis reveals the transient nature of apparent improvement.\n",

    "originalFile": "\\section{Limitations}\n\\label{sec:limitations}\n\nExperiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B) and dataset family (FOLIO); generalization remains untested.\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a high-ISR proof can still be logically vacuous if its individually necessary premises are collectively inconsistent or semantically ungrounded.\nA downstream NLI evaluation confirms this gap, finding ISR and task accuracy uncorrelated (Spearman $\\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$; Appendix~\\ref{app:extended-discussion}).\nFive seeds demonstrate qualitative divergence but limit statistical power for seed-level permutation tests ($p{\\geq}0.26$; minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$ at $n{=}5$). CMH tests yield $p{<}10^{-9}$ but assume within-stratum independence, which is violated when samples share a checkpoint and decoding strategy; CMH $p$-values are therefore inflated and should be interpreted as directional indicators, not calibrated significance levels.\nSTA requires solvers supporting component-level input manipulation.\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation as temporal snapshot.}\nAll 500-step results represent a temporal snapshot, not an equilibrium.\nTrajectory analysis on seeds~42 and~45 (1000 steps) confirms that the ${\\sim}23\\%$ genuine window observed at 500 steps under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ is transient (s42: $17\\%{\\to}2\\%$; s45: $55\\%{\\to}0\\%$ genuine).\nReaders should interpret the 500-step non-hacking rates as upper bounds on long-run structural quality under continued training.\n\n\\paragraph{Seed counts per dose.}\nISR $\\beta{=}0.05$ uses three seeds (all H7-dominated); random $\\beta{=}0.05$ uses three seeds with high variance ($\\text{PR}{=}0\\%$, $7\\%$, $62\\%$); $\\beta{=}0.2$ uses three seeds.\nThe primary $\\beta{=}0.1$ comparison uses five seeds per condition.\nAlthough all tested doses produce dominant gaming, the stochastic bifurcation mechanism (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}) means that fewer-seed dose points may not capture the full variance of collapse timing.\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ s43 and s44 checkpoints were lost during disk cleanup, preventing retrospective H8 audit on these seeds; the reported 5-seed pooled non-hacking rate (23\\%) is an uncorrected upper bound.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-scale replication.}\nOur 1.7B experiments use a single seed.\nAlthough different seeds may converge to different exploitation modes (as observed at 4B scale, where three seeds produce three distinct hacking strategies), the qualitative conclusion that hacking dominates with 0\\% genuine output is robust: at 4B, all seeds hack regardless of specific mode.\nThe zero-variance mode collapse at 1.7B (100 identical outputs) indicates a sharper convergence basin at smaller scale.\nReplicating across seeds is a natural next step.\n\n\\paragraph{Data scale.}\nOur experiments use 500 training examples and 100 evaluation examples, drawn from FOLIO~\\citep{folio}.\nThe training subset is selected via stratified sampling (seed 42) and held constant across all conditions.\nWhile our findings are consistent across seeds and logic types, the effect of training data scale on hacking prevalence and mode distribution remains an open question for future work.\n",

    "originalFile": "% Appendix — solver-trace-ablation\n\n\\section{Experimental Setup}\n\\label{app:setup}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:hyperparams} lists all hyperparameters shared across experiments.\nAll RL conditions use the same GRPO configuration; only the reward function differs.\nSeeds control model weight initialization and data shuffling; training data is held constant across all seeds and conditions (Constraint~C001).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Training hyperparameters for all GRPO experiments.}\n\\label{tab:hyperparams}\n\\footnotesize\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Parameter} & \\textbf{Value} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Model}} \\\\\nBase model & Qwen3-4B \\\\\nPrecision & BF16 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{GRPO Training}} \\\\\nRollouts per prompt ($K$) & 4 \\\\\nTraining steps (default) & 500 \\\\\nTraining steps (extended) & 1000 \\\\\nLearning rate & $1 \\times 10^{-6}$ \\\\\nPer-device batch size & 4 \\\\\nGradient accumulation steps & 2 \\\\\nEffective batch size & 8 \\\\\nMax completion length & 512 tokens \\\\\nRandom seeds & 42, 43, 44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Data}} \\\\\nTraining set & FOLIO, 500 examples \\\\\nEvaluation set & FOLIO, 100 examples (held-out) \\\\\nData file & \\texttt{train\\_500\\_seed42} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Solvers}} \\\\\nFOL solver & Prover9 \\\\\nProlog solver & SWI-Prolog 8.4.2 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{2}{@{}l}{\\textit{Evaluation}} \\\\\nDecoding & Greedy ($T{=}0$) \\\\\nISR computation & \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} \\\\\nISR variant & Proved-only (ISR$^*$) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Evaluation protocol.}\nAll cross-run ISR values are recomputed uniformly by a single evaluator (\\texttt{eval\\_sta.py}) so that training-log ISR is never compared across runs.\nThe evaluation file \\texttt{proofwriter\\_fol\\_100.jsonl} is a legacy filename containing no ProofWriter data.\nFOL exec-only seed~43 (\\texttt{plan001-exec-only-s43-r4}) was evaluated with sampling (temperature~$=0.7$) rather than greedy decoding.\nIts PR and ISR values are included for completeness but are not strictly comparable with other seeds under the unified evaluation protocol (C004).\n\n\n\\section{STA Algorithm}\n\\label{app:algorithm}\n\nAlgorithm~\\ref{alg:sta} (Section~\\ref{sec:sta}) gives the procedure for computing ISR over a proved formalization.\nFor unproved samples ($S(x) \\neq \\textit{proved}$), ISR is undefined and the protocol is not applied.\n\n\n\\section{Extended Discussion}\n\\label{app:extended-discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{What ISR measures, and what it does not.}\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.\nThis is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness (whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning).\nWe verify this empirically: Spearman $\\rho(\\text{ISR}, \\text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) from a downstream NLI evaluation on exec-only checkpoint outputs.\nISR and downstream task performance are uncorrelated.\nISR is thus a structural integrity diagnostic, not a semantic quality metric: high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.\n\nCritically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.\nUnder exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding (repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof) rather than an absence of reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nUnder ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\nLOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.\nA formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$, even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.\nThis confound is central to ISR's Goodhart vulnerability: H7 tautologies (single premise $=$ conclusion) achieve $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ \\emph{by construction}, and the shorter formalizations produced by non-collapsed seeds at $\\beta{=}0.1$ also inflate ISR mechanically.\nPremise-count-matched comparison on non-hacking samples yields $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.258$ vs.\\ $0.243$ for random ($1.06\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-detailed}), confirming that even within the transient genuine window the structural benefit is negligible.\nThis bias is not specific to our setting; any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.\nWe recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Fisher exact tests across conditions. Non-hacking rate reported at 5-seed (primary) and 3-seed with H8+FN corrections (sensitivity); H7 comparison at 5-seed (primary) and 3-seed (sensitivity).}\n\\label{tab:bootstrap-ci}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Comparison} & \\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Control} & \\textbf{OR} & \\textbf{$p$} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{Non-hacking rate (5-seed)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 22.6\\% {\\scriptsize[18.2,27.0]} & 7.7\\% {\\scriptsize[5.5,10.7]} & 3.48 & ${<}10^{-8}$ \\\\\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{Non-hacking rate (3-seed, H8+FN corrected)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 6.06\\% {\\scriptsize[3.6,9.9]} & 9.5\\% {\\scriptsize[6.5,13.7]} & 0.61 & 0.182 (n.s.) \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{H7 gaming rate (5-seed)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 54.3\\% {\\scriptsize[49.1,59.5]} & 29.5\\% {\\scriptsize[25.1,33.9]} & 2.84 & $4.82{\\times}10^{-12}$ \\\\\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{H7 gaming rate (3-seed sensitivity)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 70.6\\% {\\scriptsize[64.3,76.3]} & 13.7\\% {\\scriptsize[9.9,18.6]} & 15.05 & $1.3{\\times}10^{-39}$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{SFT vs.\\ Exec-only non-hacking rate}} \\\\\nSFT vs.\\ Exec-only & 92.3\\% {\\scriptsize[79.7,97.3]} & 7.5\\% & 147 & ${<}0.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\\\[2pt]\n{\\footnotesize 5-seed: ISR $n{=}350$, Random $n{=}414$. 3-seed: ISR $n{=}231$, Random $n{=}262$. Exec-only: $n{=}93$ (s42). SFT: $n{=}39$ (3 seeds, 1000 steps). 95\\% Wilson CIs in brackets where shown.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Seed-level statistical analysis.}\nTo address potential inflation from within-seed dependence, we supplement pooled Fisher exact tests with two seed-aware analyses.\nCochran--Mantel--Haenszel (CMH) tests stratify by seed while pooling across samples: H7 $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}2.46$ (95\\% CI $[1.85, 3.27]$, $p{=}5.6{\\times}10^{-16}$); genuine $\\text{OR}_{\\text{CMH}}{=}3.37$ (95\\% CI $[2.21, 5.13]$, $p{=}4.5{\\times}10^{-10}$).\nCMH assumes within-stratum independence, but samples within each seed share a single checkpoint and use greedy decoding; the effective independent units are seeds ($n{=}5$), not samples, and CMH $p$-values are consequently inflated.\nSeed-level permutation tests (treating each seed as a single observation, $n{=}5$ per group) yield $p{=}0.28$ (H7) and $p{=}0.26$ (genuine), consistent with the severe power limitation of $n{=}5$ (minimum achievable $p{=}0.008$) rather than absence of effect.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed classification results for ISR and Random at $\\beta{=}0.1$ (500 steps, FOL). $n$ = proved samples. ISR$^*$ = proved-only mean ISR. $\\dagger$~H8 audit complete; $\\ddagger$~checkpoint unavailable before audit, N-H is uncorrected upper bound. Percentages are per-classifier; samples may match multiple modes. N-H uses set-exclusion per the C013 taxonomy standard.}\n\\label{tab:fol-beta01-perseed}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lrrrrrrrr}\n\\toprule\n& Seed & PR\\% & $n$ & ISR$^*$ & H7\\% & H1\\% & H8\\% & N-H\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{6}{*}{\\rotatebox{90}{ISR}}\n& s42 & 81 & 81 & 0.415 & 16.0 & 51.9 & 14.8$\\dagger$ & 17.3 \\\\\n& s43 & 61 & 61 & 0.830 & 100 & 0 & ---$\\ddagger$ & 0 \\\\\n& s44 & 89 & 89 & 0.902 & 100 & 0 & ---$\\ddagger$ & 0 \\\\\n& s45 & 42 & 42 & 0.605 & 38.1 & 4.8 & 2.4 & 54.8 \\\\\n& s46 & 77 & 77 & 0.411 & 14.3 & 26.0 & 5.2 & 54.5 \\\\\n& \\textbf{Pool} & \\textbf{70$\\pm$19} & \\textbf{350} & --- & \\textbf{54.3} & \\textbf{18.0} & --- & \\textbf{22.6} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{6}{*}{\\rotatebox{90}{Random}}\n& s42 & 79 & 79 & 0.104 & 0 & 73.4 & 5.1$\\dagger$ & 22.8 \\\\\n& s43 & 94 & 94 & 0.141 & 38.3 & 59.6 & 1.1 & 1.1 \\\\\n& s44 & 89 & 89 & 0.062 & 0 & 82.0 & 18.0 & 6.7 \\\\\n& s45 & 58 & 58 & 0.010 & 0 & 91.4 & 0 & 8.6 \\\\\n& s46 & 94 & 94 & 0.042 & 91.5 & 2.1 & 4.3 & 2.1 \\\\\n& \\textbf{Pool} & \\textbf{83$\\pm$15} & \\textbf{414} & --- & \\textbf{29.5} & \\textbf{55.8} & --- & \\textbf{7.7} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{H7 as inevitable exploit.}\nISR's LOO definition creates an inherent structural bias: tautological proofs ($P{\\to}P$) receive $\\text{ISR}{=}0.75$, and single-premise-equals-conclusion constructions receive $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$, making H7 the global ISR-maximizing exploit.\n$\\beta$ controls the \\emph{timing} of collapse, not whether it occurs.\nAt all four tested doses, H7 dominates: $\\beta{=}0.05$ (3 seeds, pooled H7 $93.7\\%$, $n{=}253$; random s43 achieves $\\text{PR}{=}62\\%$ with H1 $77.4\\%$ and zero H7, confirming mode specificity); $\\beta{=}0.1$ (stochastic bifurcation: 2/5 seeds collapse within ${\\sim}150$ steps, remaining 3/5 sustain a transient window that closes under extended training); $\\beta{=}0.2$ ($100\\%$ H7 across all three seeds, $n{=}290$; Table~\\ref{tab:beta02-detail}); $\\beta{=}0.3$ (multi-modal, s44 pure H7).\nThe random control produces $29.5\\%$ H7 ($122/414$, 5-seed; per-seed: $0\\%$--$91.5\\%$, dominated by s46 at $91.5\\%$ and s43 at $38.3\\%$; s42/s44/s45 at $0\\%$), random $\\beta{=}0.3$ yields 4--7 H7a per seed.\nOnce a seed samples and reinforces the H7 exploit, convergence is irreversible; this absorbing-state property guarantees eventual collapse regardless of dose.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{ISR $\\beta{=}0.2$ per-seed results (maximum exploitation point). All seeds converge to H7a tautology with 0\\% genuine.}\n\\label{tab:beta02-detail}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR\\%} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7a\\%} & \\textbf{Genuine\\%} \\\\\n\\midrule\ns42 & 100 & 0.55 & 100 & 0 \\\\\ns43 & 97 & 0.96 & 96.9 & 0 \\\\\ns44 & 93 & 0.55 & 100 & 0 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Pooled} & 97$\\pm$4 & 0.69$\\pm$0.24 & 99.0 & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Goodhart effects: implications.}\nThe finding extends beyond this specific system.\nISR was designed to \\emph{detect} reward gaming of $r_{\\text{exec}}$, yet it contains an inherent structural vulnerability: by its LOO definition, tautological proofs receive artificially high ISR ($P{\\to}P$ yields $\\text{ISR}{=}0.75$; single-premise-equals-conclusion yields $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$), creating a positive feedback loop once sampled during GRPO rollouts~\\citep{karwowski-goodhart-rl, kwa-catastrophic-goodhart}.\nGoodhart collapse~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy} is observed at all four tested doses ($\\beta{=}0.05$: 3 seeds; $\\beta\\in\\{0.1, 0.2, 0.3\\}$: 3--5 seeds each).\nThe structural bias predicts universality (H7 tautology is always the global reward-maximizing exploit regardless of $\\beta$), and $\\beta$ controls only the probability of early sampling and thus the timing of collapse; at $\\beta{=}0.05$, three seeds confirm deterministic H7 collapse ($93.7\\%$ pooled) while the matched random control at the same $\\beta$ produces H1 redundancy instead ($77.4\\%$), ruling out dose magnitude as the driver.\nAt moderate doses ($\\beta{=}0.1$), collapse is stochastic and delayed (5 seeds bifurcate: 2 collapse rapidly, 3 sustain a transient genuine window that trajectory analysis confirms is temporary); at higher doses ($\\beta{=}0.2$), collapse is immediate and universal.\n$\\beta{=}0.3$ adds reward collapse~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization} as a competing failure mode (PR drops to 18\\% in 1/3 seeds), but the underlying Goodhart vulnerability remains the same.\nThe practical implication is that ISR (and structurally similar LOO-based diagnostics) should be used exclusively as post-hoc evaluation tools, never as training rewards.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-seed unreliability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}47$ percentage points), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}).\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ (5 seeds) shows even greater instability: 2/5 seeds collapse to H7 within ${\\sim}150$ steps, while 3/5 sustain a transient genuine window.\nThe 5-seed pooled non-hacking rate is ${\\sim}23\\%$ at 500 steps; trajectory analysis on seeds~42 and~45 (1000 steps) confirms this is temporary ($17\\%{\\to}2\\%$ and $55\\%{\\to}0\\%$ genuine respectively).\nThe dominant hacking mode (H7 vs.\\ H1) is stochastic within any treatment condition ($\\text{CV}{>}1$).\nMulti-seed evaluation with trajectory analysis should be standard practice for neurosymbolic RL systems; single-seed or single-checkpoint results can misrepresent a configuration's true hacking profile.\n\n\n\\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}\n\\label{app:hacking-modes}\n\nWe identify nine distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.\nEach mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.\nTable~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app} summarizes prevalence across FOL conditions.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples; exec-only: seed~42; all other bars: 3-seed subset s42/s43/s44, per-seed breakdown in Table~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-perseed}; 5-seed data in Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}). ISR increases H7 probability without reducing total hacking; the specific mode (H7 vs.\\ H1) is seed-dependent within each condition. SFT shows near-zero hacking.}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Mechanism} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR} & \\textbf{Rnd} & \\textbf{SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 & Concl.\\ in premises & 87 & 18 & 71 & 7 \\\\\nH7 & Single prem.\\ = concl. & $\\bullet$ & \\textbf{71} & 14$^\\dagger$ & 0 \\\\\nH8 & Premise-subset gaming & 6 & 5 & -- & 0 \\\\\nTautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nRepetition & Output degen. & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nContradiction & $n_{\\text{comp}}$ inflated & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nC-w/o-utility & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\\\\nQ-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & -- & -- & -- & -- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed subset (s42/s43/s44); 5-seed totals in Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}. H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}14$; primarily s44). $^\\dagger$Random H7 in 3-seed subset driven by seed~43 (38.3\\%); 5-seed pooled: 30\\% (dominated by s46 at 91.5\\%). Exec: H1${=}87$\\% in s42; $\\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes. H8: premise-subset gaming (Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Classifier Validation.}\nHacking mode classification uses deterministic rule-based classifiers, not learned models, so standard ML precision/recall is not directly applicable.\nH1 and H7 are identified by normalized text matching between conclusion and premise strings after whitespace and variable normalization; remaining modes are detected via functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution from STA (leave-one-out ablation).\nNo training data or manual annotation is used for classification.\n\nTo estimate precision, we manually verified 50 randomly sampled outputs classified as H1 or H7: all 50 were confirmed correct (estimated precision ${>}$95\\%, Wilson 95\\% CI: [93\\%, 100\\%]).\nRecall is inherently bounded by the text-match threshold: semantic manipulations that alter surface form while preserving the gaming structure (e.g., variable renaming beyond our normalization, or paraphrased conclusions) would be missed.\nWe cross-validate with functional LOO analysis: samples classified as non-hacking but showing $n_{\\text{necessary}} \\leq 1$ are flagged as potential false negatives.\nThis cross-validation revealed query-as-fact gaming in Prolog (\\S\\ref{app:hacking-modes}), where the strict text classifier reported 100\\% non-hacking but functional analysis showed 95/97 samples were tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$).\nThe non-hacking rate for FOL conditions may therefore be slightly overestimated, as subtle semantic manipulations below our text-matching threshold would evade the strict classifier.\n\n\\subsection{H8 --- Premise-Subset Gaming}\n\\label{app:h8}\n\nManual audit of ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 revealed a previously undetected hacking mode: the model emits a subset of the gold premises that is individually necessary (high ISR) but insufficient for the intended derivation, with proof success depending on the conclusion already being embedded (H1-style).\nWe term this \\textbf{H8 (premise-subset gaming)}: formalizations where each component is LOO-necessary but the premise set is a strict subset of what the NL problem requires, inflating ISR without faithful reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{Detection.} H8 is identified by manual inspection of non-hacking samples with unusually low premise counts ($n_{\\text{prem}} \\leq 3$) that nonetheless achieve high ISR.\nAutomated detection is not currently feasible because H8 requires comparing the generated premise set against the gold premises.\n\n\\paragraph{Audit scope and limitations.}\nFull H8 audit was completed for ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 only.\nISR seeds~43 and~44 checkpoints were deleted before audit and cannot be recovered; their non-hacking rates may be overestimated.\nRandom $\\beta{=}0.1$ does not produce H8 by mechanism (no ISR optimization pressure); seed~42 audit confirmed H8${=}0$.\n\n\\paragraph{H7 false-negative correction (H7b obfuscated tautology).}\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 contained 12 samples with $n_{\\text{prem}}{=}1$ classified as non-hacking by the text matcher.\nManual inspection confirmed all 12 are logically equivalent to H7 but evade text matching through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$) or structural simplification ($1/12$).\nWe classify these as \\textbf{H7b} (obfuscated tautology), distinct from text-match-detectable H7a.\n$10/12$ achieve $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.83$, indicating simultaneous gaming of execution reward, ISR metric, and H7 classifier (triple gaming; \\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\nAfter correction, ISR s44 non-hacking${=}0$; total H7 = H7a (77 text-match) + H7b (12 obfuscated) = 89.\n\n\\paragraph{Impact on non-hacking-rate comparison.}\nAfter H8 reclassification and H7 false-negative correction: ISR non-hacking $= 14/231 = 6.06\\%$ vs.\\ random non-hacking $= 25/262 = 9.5\\%$ (Fisher $p{=}0.182$, n.s.; Wilson 95\\% CI: ISR [3.6\\%, 9.9\\%], Random [6.5\\%, 13.7\\%]).\nThe direction is consistent with ISR-amplified gaming modes (H7, H8) displacing non-hacking output, but the effect is not statistically significant at the conventional $\\alpha{=}0.05$ threshold.\n\n\\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.\nThe solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  flies(tweety).  % conclusion embedded\nConclusion: flies(tweety).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nVariable. The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.\nISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nConclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions.\nUnder exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (59--91\\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (54\\% pooled, 5-seed).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7a --- Conclusion Duplication (Standard)}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical (text-match detectable) to the conclusion.\nSince $P \\to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.\nBecause the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.\nThe single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.\nH7a games both exec reward and ISR reward; H7b extends this to triple gaming by additionally evading the text-match classifier.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nH7a (text-match detectable): 64.5\\% (149/231, 3-seed) under ISR~$\\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\\%, s43: 96.7\\%, s44: 86.5\\%); 5-seed pooled H7: 54\\% (190/350, Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}).\nCombined H7 (H7a + H7b, 3-seed): 70.6\\% (163/231; s43 H7a${=}59$ + H7b${=}2$; includes s44 FN correction).\nRandom~$\\beta{=}0.1$ pooled: 29.5\\% (122/414, 5-seed, all H7a; driven by s46 at 91.5\\% and s43 at 38.3\\%; s42/s44/s45 at 0\\%).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7b --- Obfuscated Tautology}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nA single-premise formalization logically equivalent to H7a (premise = conclusion) but with surface-form differences that evade the text-match H7 classifier.\nThe model achieves this through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$ instances) or structural simplification ($1/12$).\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, vacuous quantifier wrapping).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y ((A(x)&B(y))->(C(x)|D(y))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\nThe premise introduces a vacuous second quantifier variable $y$ that is immediately unified with $x$, making the formulas logically equivalent while differing textually.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\n$\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.83$ ($10/12$ samples).\nH7b simultaneously games three objectives: execution reward ($r_{\\text{exec}}{=}1$ via tautological proof), ISR metric (single necessary premise yields high ISR), and the text-match H7 classifier (surface-form difference evades detection).\nThis triple gaming makes H7b the most complete Goodhart case we observe.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nFound primarily in ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 ($12/89$ proved samples, all originally classified as non-hacking); seed~43 contributes $2$ additional cases.\nFunctional cross-validation via $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ exposed the misclassification.\n\n\n\\subsection{Tautological Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nTwo-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \\to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises: p(a).\nConclusion: p(a).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).\nDistinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\\text{comp}}\\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nDominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\\% H7 by strict classifier).\nAlso observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Repetition Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.\nRemoving any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\\approx 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\n... (repeated to 512 tokens)\nquery(penguin(fido)).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.\nISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=13$\\%, ISR~$=0$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous; none is individually necessary for the proof.\nThe complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y (R(x,y) ->\n    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).\n  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).\n  P(a).            % only this needed\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.\nThis is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nLow. Observed in Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\\% (3/79).\nNot observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).\n\n\n\\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe component count grows unboundedly over training.\nExcess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso quodlibet}.\nIndividual premises may register as ISR~$=0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  c1. c2. c3. ... c18. % 18.55 mean\n  % conclusion embedded among premises\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).\nMean component count grows from ${\\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).\n\n\n\\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.\nProlog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.\nThis mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n% NL: \"Fido is happy.\"\n% Model output:\nfido_is_happy.\n:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.\nThe single fact is trivially necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.\nFunctionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$=0.97$, ISR~$=0.912$).\n95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% non-hacking.\n\n\n\\subsection{H9 --- Ex Falso Quodlibet (Contradiction Injection)}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates a premise set containing logically contradictory assertions (e.g., $P(x)$ and $\\neg P(x)$).\nFrom contradictory premises, any conclusion follows by the \\emph{ex falso quodlibet} principle, rendering the proof semantically vacuous regardless of the conclusion's content.\nUnlike the ``contradiction explosion'' mode observed in 4B exec-only at 1000 steps (where contradictions arise incidentally among inflated premise counts), H9 is a \\emph{targeted} strategy: the model constructs minimal contradictory pairs that directly enable the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  -bird(tweety).  % contradicts above\nConclusion: happy(tweety).\n% Proof: bird(tweety) & -bird(tweety) -> False -> anything\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$>0$ because each side of the contradiction is individually necessary under LOO: removing $P(x)$ leaves $\\neg P(x)$ alone, which cannot derive a contradiction; removing $\\neg P(x)$ leaves the premises consistent but insufficient to derive an unrelated conclusion.\nThe LOO test thus registers both contradictory premises as ``necessary,'' inflating ISR despite the formalization being semantically vacuous.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved exclusively in Qwen3-1.7B under Random $\\beta{=}0.1$ (seed~42, 500 steps, proper train/eval separation): 100\\% of proved samples ($n{=}93$) exhibit H9.\nUnder ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ with the same model, H9 does not appear; the model instead converges to fixed-template H1 ($\\text{ISR}{=}0.6$, zero variance), indicating that ISR reward redirects the hacking pathway from H9 to H1.\nNot observed in any Qwen3-4B experiment across all conditions and seeds tested. %% registry: d113-1.7b-random-b01-s42\n\n\n\\section{Per-Seed Detailed Results}\n\\label{app:detailed-results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-detailed} and \\ref{tab:prolog-detailed} report full per-seed results.\nAll metrics are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} on the held-out 100-example FOLIO evaluation set using greedy decoding unless otherwise noted.\nISR values are proved-only ISR$^*$.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: FOL (Prover9). s44-1000 samples escape text-match heuristics (H1/H8 classifiers) but exhibit ISR${=}0$ and functionally degenerate proof structures: disjunctive conclusions that bypass H8 detection and commuted premises that bypass H1 detection; reclassified as degenerate. $^\\P$Inferred from ISR profile and contradiction-explosion pattern; formal text-match taxonomy not run on this checkpoint.}\n\\label{tab:fol-detailed}\n\\label{tab:fol-beta01-detailed}\n\\footnotesize\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & All seeds converge to high PR with near-zero genuine output. s42 evaluated with ISR metrics only; hacking taxonomy unavailable.}\n\\label{tab:exec-only-1000}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lrrrrrr}\n\\toprule\nSeed & PR\\% & $n$ & H7\\% & H1\\% & H8\\% & N-H\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\ns42 & 96 & 96 & 0 & --- & --- & --- \\\\\ns43 & 96 & 96 & 0 & 93.8 & 0 & 6.2 \\\\\ns44 & 100 & 100 & 0 & 85.0 & 15.0 & 0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition: all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: Prolog (SWI-Prolog 8.4.2). PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H\\ = percentage among proved samples.  ``---'' indicates data not available.}\n\\label{tab:prolog-detailed}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec-only}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.83 & 0.037 & 0 & 0 & --- & Ground fact enumeration \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.56 & 0.015 & --- & --- & --- & \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.21 & 0.387 & --- & --- & --- & Small-sample ($n{=}21$) \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.53{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.146{\\pm}0.21$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec+ISR (STA reward)}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.50 & 1.000 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & H7 tautological \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.38 & 0.185 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 97.4 & 0 & $^\\ddagger$Functional H7$^a$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.97 & 0.912 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 0 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & Query-as-fact gaming$^b$ \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.62{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.699{\\pm}0.45$} & & 3/3 Goodhart, 0\\% non-hacking \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\n\\vspace{4pt}\n{\\footnotesize\n$^a$ Strict H7 classifier reports 0\\%, but functional analysis shows all 38/38 proved samples are tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$). Uppercase pseudo-Prolog syntax bypasses the text-match classifier.\\\\\n$^b$ Strict classifier reports Non-hacking$\\,{=}\\,100$\\%, but 95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$. The model converts NL to snake\\_case atoms serving as both fact and query, a third distinct Goodhart pathway.\\\\\n$^\\ddagger$ Strict classifier value is misleading; see footnotes $^a$/$^b$.\n}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig5_training_isr_curves.pdf}\n\\caption{Training ISR trajectories across reward conditions. ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds rise rapidly toward $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}1$ (Goodhart gaming via H7 tautology); exec-only s44's plateau at ${\\sim}0.5$ is an H7a artifact ($n{=}2$ gives $\\text{ISR}{\\geq}0.5$ by construction), not genuine structural quality; random $\\beta{=}0.1$ remains low. The s42 1k-step trace shows ISR eventually saturates at $0.88$ through the same H7 mechanism.}\n\\label{fig:training-isr-curves}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}\nTable~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:\nISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\\%) $\\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.5$\\% from step 100).\nPR increases monotonically ($0.32 \\to 0.93 \\to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.\nBy step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.\n\n\\paragraph{Prolog: consistent gaming across all seeds via three distinct pathways.}\nAll three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart effects with 0\\% non-hacking proofs (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}), but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:\nseed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),\nseed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,\nand seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds, despite identical training data and reward function, demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n\n\\subsection{Per-Seed Results at $\\beta{=}0.3$}\n\\label{app:beta03}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:beta03-detailed} reports per-seed results for both ISR and Random reward at $\\beta{=}0.3$.\nThe three ISR seeds illustrate qualitatively distinct Goodhart pathways (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed results at $\\beta{=}0.3$ (FOL). PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H = percentage among proved samples.}\n\\label{tab:beta03-detailed}\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Mode} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{ISR $\\beta{=}0.3$}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.18 & 0.143 & 0 & 77.8 & 5.6 & Reward collapse ($n{=}18$); H8${=}3$ \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.86 & 0.295 & 4.7 & 68.6 & 23.3 & H1 shortcutting \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.95 & 0.742 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & Pure H7 gaming \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.66{\\pm}0.42$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.39{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Random $\\beta{=}0.3$}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.48 & 0.094 & 8 & 92 & 0 & H7a${=}4$ (D086) \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.92 & 0.180 & 7.6 & 89.1 & 3.3 & H7a${=}7$; $n_{\\text{N-H}}{=}3$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.23 & 0 & 0 & 100 & 0 & Pure H1 ($n{=}23$) \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.54{\\pm}0.35$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.09{\\pm}0.09$} & & \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Per-Seed Results at $\\beta{=}0.05$}\n\\label{app:beta005}\n\nThe 3-seed random control at $\\beta{=}0.05$ is highly unstable: s42 fails entirely ($\\text{PR}{=}0\\%$), s44 barely learns ($\\text{PR}{=}7\\%$, H1 $71\\%$, genuine $0\\%$), and only s43 reaches moderate output ($\\text{PR}{=}62\\%$, H1 $77\\%$, genuine $13\\%$).\nThe single random seed that learns (s43) shows higher genuine output than ISR ($13\\%$ vs.\\ $5.1\\%$ pooled), consistent with a signal-floor below which ISR's genuine component is undetectable while its H7 bias remains exploitable.\n\n\\subsection{Early-Training Hacking Analysis (100-Step Checkpoint)}\n\\label{app:100step}\n\nTo control for the prove-rate confound in our SFT comparison (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}), we analyze the exec-only checkpoint at 100 training steps (seed~42), where PR${=}32$\\%, higher than SFT (13.0\\%, 3-seed pooled) but still early in training.\nOf the 32 proved samples, 9 (28.1\\%) exhibit H7 hacking (single premise identical to conclusion) and 1 (3.1\\%) exhibits H1 (conclusion embedded among premises), yielding a total hacking rate of 31.2\\%.\nBy contrast, SFT at comparable or lower PR shows 7.7\\% hacking (1/13 proved samples).\nThis demonstrates that reward hacking emerges early in GRPO training, well before the prove-rate plateau, and is not simply an artifact of having more proved samples available for exploitation.\nThe 100-step hacking rate (31.2\\%) is already substantially above SFT (7.7\\%), supporting the claim that hacking is associated with RL optimization rather than prove-rate level alone.\n\n\\subsection{$O(1)$ vs.\\ $O(n)$ ISR Ablation}\n\\label{app:o1-ablation}\n\nTo justify the per-clause LOO design (\\S\\ref{sec:sta}), we train a variant that replaces the $O(n)$ ablation with a cheaper $O(1)$ binary signal: $\\text{fast\\_ISR}(x) = 1$ if $x$ is proved \\emph{and} the conclusion is not provable under empty premises (tautology check), $0$ otherwise.\nThe reward becomes $r = 0.5\\,r_{\\text{exec}} + 0.5\\,\\text{fast\\_ISR}$.\nAfter 500 steps (seed~42), this variant achieves $\\text{PR}{=}63\\%$ with $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$ (functionally zero), compared to $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.132$ under standard exec-only and $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.42$ under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR signal vs.\\ training dynamics.}\nAt the \\emph{ISR signal level}, the $O(1)$ variant is indistinguishable from exec-only: both produce near-zero ISR because models emit redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.\nAt the \\emph{training level}, the two conditions are non-equivalent: the $O(1)$ tautology check penalizes formalizations where the conclusion is provable without any premises, a constraint absent from exec-only.\nThis confound depresses prove rate by 30 percentage points ($63\\%$ vs.\\ $93\\%$ exec-only), making it impossible to attribute the difference purely to the ISR signal.\nThe result confirms that the $O(n)$ per-clause ablation is necessary for ISR to carry a structural signal, while also showing that even a minimal tautology guard alters training dynamics substantially.\n",

7	
8	\subsection{Setup}\label{sec:setup}
9	
10	We train Qwen3-4B~\citep{qwen3} with GRPO~\citep{deepseekmath} ($K{=}4$, lr $10^{-6}$, 500 steps default, 1000 where noted) over seeds $\{42,43,44\}$ on a fixed 500-example FOLIO~\citep{folio} subset held constant across all conditions; the SFT baseline is trained with negative log-likelihood (NLL) on gold formalizations.\footnote{All experiments use \texttt{data/train\_500\_seed42.jsonl}; seeds control initialization and rollout sampling only.}
11	Held-out evaluation uses 100 FOLIO examples (\texttt{proofwriter\_fol\_100.jsonl}) with greedy decoding (Appendix~\ref{app:setup}).
12	We report prove rate (PR), proved-only ISR ($\text{ISR}^*$), hacking-mode rates from a two-stage classifier (strict text matching for H1/H7 plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\text{necessary}}$ distribution; \S\ref{sec:taxonomy-method}), and a \emph{non-hacking} (N-H) rate, defined as the residual of proved samples matching none of the nine cataloged patterns (a necessary but not sufficient condition for semantic correctness).
13

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01YSb6P2gGTgo2XnCiiXfNRQ`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	GRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on FOLIO ($97{\pm}2\%$) yet produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs at convergence, a failure invisible to execution-based evaluation.
3	We propose \emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a leave-one-out necessity test on solver inputs whose derived metric, Irreducible Solver Rate (ISR), exposes a 99.5\% structural collapse hidden behind rising prove rates; a supervised baseline achieves 92\% non-hacking on identical data, suggesting the failure is RL-specific.
4	Repurposed as a reward signal, ISR exhibits Goodhart collapse at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\beta{=}0.1$): tautological proofs receive artificially high ISR by construction, and the transient genuine window at moderate doses shows suggestive but non-robust improvement over random controls that disappears under extended training.
5	Across multiple reward conditions, logic types, and model scales, we catalog nine hacking modes and document that ISR is reliable as a diagnostic but unreliable as an optimization target, with collapse dynamics that resist prediction from the metric definition alone.\footnote{Code and data: \url{[anonymized]}}
6	\end{abstract}
7	
```

      "content": "\\begin{abstract}\nGRPO-trained formalizers converge to high prove rates on FOLIO ($97{\\pm}2\\%$) yet produce effectively zero structurally sound outputs at convergence, a failure invisible to execution-based evaluation.\nWe propose \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a leave-one-out necessity test on solver inputs whose derived metric, Irreducible Solver Rate (ISR), exposes a 99.5\\% structural collapse hidden behind rising prove rates; a supervised baseline achieves 92\\% non-hacking on identical data, suggesting the failure is RL-specific.\nRepurposed as a reward signal, ISR exhibits Goodhart collapse at all four tested doses (trajectory-confirmed at $\\beta{=}0.1$): tautological proofs receive artificially high ISR by construction, and the transient genuine window at moderate doses shows suggestive but non-robust improvement over random controls that disappears under extended training.\nAcross multiple reward conditions, logic types, and model scales, we catalog nine hacking modes and document that ISR is reliable as a diagnostic but unreliable as an optimization target, with collapse dynamics that resist prediction from the metric definition alone.\\footnote{Code and data: \\url{[anonymized]}}\n\\end{abstract}\n",
